In Superpairs it plays in turns, counts what is left on the board to deduce hidden tiles, and takes money before XP; Skip Grand XP Bottles, Skip Guardian Pets and Skip Metaphysical Serums (three switches) leave those for the very end, only if clicks remain; Explore Before Claiming (off by default) also holds back every reward worth up to Hold Back Up To (0-3,000,000 coins in 100,000 steps, 1,000,000 by default, priced like the profit tracker) until the board is known, then goes back for them most valuable first, while anything worth more is claimed at once (held-back rewards are still money: their partners are looked for before XP, and once the clicks left would no longer cover them they are taken first); the Nadeshiko Dye, Superpairs' mega-rare drop, always comes first whatever the settings: once the board shows it, nothing else is claimed until the dye is, an Instant Find included (it is never mistaken for an XP tile); T7 Drops First (off by default) does the same for a T7 book (an enchant at its true max of VII, the level Enchant Colors paints Perfect), after the dye; powerups work as on Hypixel (a powerup never takes a turn's click, +Clicks is a free space, Instant Finds stack); when the board's clicks run out it presses Escape once, which takes you to the rewards to claim. Only **Escape** or the **Emergency Cancel Key** (Home) stops a run - no other key, click or scroll does, and opening chat only pauses it (it does not reopen the table over chat). The press that stops it is swallowed, so the board stays open, and chat says what stopped it. You can then finish the board by hand: the solver follows your own clicks from the step the board is really at (a click the run had decided but not sent is given back), so its highlights keep up and a new Start ETable carries on from there - see [Which jar do I download?](#installing) below.
- Experimentation Table Profit Tracker — logs every claimed experiment (game, clicks, rewards, XP, bits) with coin values and running totals, plus a max-clicks chat notice whether you are using the solver, the Auto E-Table, or both. Its tab has the on/off switch and an Open /profit button; the totals and the reset are on the /profit screen. On by default
- Profit Trackers (/profit) — one command for every profit tracker. `/profit` opens a hub with a card per tracker (Dungeon/Croesus, Experimentation Table), each with a live one-line summary; click a card to open that tracker, and Back or Escape returns to the hub. `/profit <name>` opens one directly and tab-completes: `croesus` (or `dungeon`), `etable` (or `experiments`); an unknown name lists the valid ones in chat. `/croesus` and `/croesus profit` still open the dungeon tracker. The Experimentation Table tracker has its own screen (drawn with the same panels as the dungeon one) and an Open /profit button in its tab. Every claim is recorded against the Minecraft account (by its UUID, so a renamed account keeps its profit), Skyblock profile and mod profile it was made on. Profit is kept separate per Minecraft account: **Show Profit** (in the Croesus and Experiments settings) is This Account by default, which counts only what the account you are logged in on recorded, or All Accounts, which adds every account together. **Profit Data** (beside it) is Shared Across Profiles by default, so switching mod profile never hides your profit, or Per Mod Profile, where each mod profile shows only the profit recorded while it was the active one (renaming a profile keeps its profit); switching either way moves and loses nothing. Both settings are the same in every mod profile and are not part of one. Each tracker screen has switches for the account (This Account, All Accounts, then each other account with profit, by name), the Skyblock profile and the mod profile (left click steps forward, right click back; All on each; under Per Mod Profile the mod profile switch shows the current one and does not step) plus All Profit, which shows everything ever tracked (every account; under Per Mod Profile, everything in this profile). Reset Totals turns red and asks once more ("Reset <what is selected>?"), and resets only what the switches select; the dungeon log keeps every claim in its file. Claims from before accounts were kept go to the account that recorded them when its name is known (the account you are on, or one of Prism's saved accounts), and otherwise to the account you are on the first time this version reads the tracker; a claim with no mod profile goes to the current one. Claims with no Skyblock profile show as ? on that switch. Client-side only, so it works the same on Hypixel
- How Lucky Are You (RNG Tracker) — every drop you get against the drops the odds say you should have got. `/luck` (or `/profit luck`, or the How Lucky Are You card in Misc) opens the screen: headline cards for your **Overall Luck** (the average luck percentile of every tracked drop: 50% is exactly the odds, 80% means luckier than 80 of 100 players who did the same), drops got against expected, how many drops are above and below their odds, and attempts; the drop list (got / expected, green when you are ahead); and for a clicked drop its chance, attempts, expected, how lucky you are on it, the attempts by which half and 90% of players have one, and a **calculator** (Attempts and Magic Find steppers: left click adds a step, right click takes one away, Step picks 1 to 10,000; Use My Stats puts your own numbers back). Switches: **This Profile / All Profiles / Other Mod Users**, a group filter (Dungeon Chests, Slayers, Mobs, Sea Creatures, Kuudra, Crystal Nucleus, Experiments, Dyes, Other), the sort, and **Chest Odds** (the wiki's chance with No Bonuses, or with S+ Max Bonuses). What it counts: **dungeon chests** from your Croesus claim log, so every chest you ever claimed counts from day one (one attempt per claimed chest of that floor and type; a claim logged with floor Unknown takes the one floor its items fit, or counts under Unknown with its items but no odds); **slayer kills** when Hypixel says SLAYER QUEST COMPLETE / NICE! SLAYER BOSS SLAIN with the boss and tier on your sidebar, each kill adding every drop's chance at the Magic Find of that kill (the last Magic Find Hypixel printed on a drop line, or the Manual value in the tab; Magic Find raises slayer drops under 5% as the wiki's Slayer page says), leaving out drops above your slayer level (read from Hypixel's "Zombie Slayer LVL 7" line after each kill) and raising the drop your slayer RNG Meter is set to from 1x to 3x as the meter fills (read when you open that meter in /rng; 100% once full); **mob and sea creature kills** (Mob Kills: a mob you hit that dies, or any named mob dying within 8 blocks, or Off), one attempt at that mob's wiki drop table, with the **Looting** of the weapon you hold (each level +15%, applied before Magic Find, never on pets) and your Magic Find (on drops still under 5% after Looting); **Kuudra Paid Chests** when you open one (its tier from the key, its items as drops; "You cannot afford this!" undoes it); **Crystal Nucleus** loot bundles from the loot message; **Superpairs** claims (Metaphysical: each of the 37 ultra-rare books at 1% / 37; Supreme and Transcendent are counted with no odds, the wiki gives none); **drop lines** (RARE / VERY RARE / CRAZY RARE / INSANE / PRAY TO RNGESUS / RNGESUS INCARNATE / PET DROP!), never a player's chat line; and **dye pickups**: a dye landing in your inventory with no menu open. An item you dropped and picked back up, one that left your inventory in the last 10 minutes, one whose item UUID you already had, anything gained while a menu is open (trades, chests, Auction House, storage) and the dye that lands right after its own drop line never count. The odds table ships with the mod (2,700+ drops: every Catacombs and Master Mode reward chest, all six slayer bosses by tier, every mob and sea creature page on the wiki, Kuudra paid chests by tier, the Crystal Nucleus bundle, the Superpairs ultra-rare books and every dye that drops), read from hypixelskyblock.minecraft.wiki. A mob drop has odds when Hypixel announces it (Rare, Legendary or RNGesus drops, and anything under 5%), so drops as small as 1/10 count when they come with a drop line; drops that go to your sacks with no line are listed without odds. Garden pests are farming and are not in it. `/luck calc <attempts> <magicFind> <drop>` prints the expected drops and the chance of at least one for any drop ("how many Handles in 10,000 F7 runs?"). **Drop Chat Line** prints your luck on a drop the moment it is counted. **Share My Luck** (off by default) sends your overall luck and top drops to the other mod users in your relay room, and only while Show Me As A Mod User is on; you see theirs under Other Mod Users while See Other Mod Users is on. Records live in the shared store, so every instance on the PC adds to the same numbers
- Storage Overlay — see the contents of every Ender Chest page and Backpack live, right alongside your open inventory. Always centered across the top of the screen at any window size or GUI scale (you can still drag its height in the HUD editor). Three themes: Amber (the original dark look), Dark (neutral grays, no orange) and Light. A Scan All button beside the grid (under Search) opens every real Ender Chest page and backpack in turn, at an unhurried, slightly varied pace, so the grid holds all of them - it reads Hypixel's Storage menu first and never opens a locked page or an empty backpack slot. While it runs the button shows its progress ("Stop 3/8"); press it again, or Escape, to stop
- Bug Report — `/killer560 bugreport` builds one zip you can drag straight into Discord: your latest log, every one of the mod's config files, and a report of your mod/Minecraft/loader versions, which jar you are running, your OS, Java and memory, and every mod you have loaded. **Credentials are stripped before anything goes in.** Your session-login and account-proxy files are left out entirely rather than redacted, and in everything else any value whose name looks like a token, secret, password, auth, session or cookie is replaced with [redacted] - verified against a file containing six different fake credentials, all six removed, with keybinds and normal settings left untouched. Log lines are scrubbed the same way, and as a last pass every file in the zip has anything shaped like a credential removed whatever it is called (an API key in a web address or header, a Microsoft or Discord token, a session id), and your Windows user folder shows as <home>. It tells you where the zip is and opens the folder
- Dungeon Sim — **work in progress, not part of a release yet.** A clear-practice dungeon you join from a Dungeon Sim button on the Multiplayer screen. It connects to nothing: it opens a flat local world this mod owns, and everything in it is client side. The button opens one wide menu titled DUNGEON SIM with its pages in a column down the left - **Create a New Map** (the Map Designer), **Previous Runs**, **Load a Room** and **Route Practice** (see Dungeon Sim Room Filters), the open one lit in the accent - with **Back** at the bottom of the column, and the page you pick fills the rest; there is no in-between "pick what to practice" page. The first time you open it in a game session it opens on Create a New Map; after that it reopens on the page you were last on (remembered until the game closes, not across restarts), and a drawing on the designer is kept while you look at the other pages. The column stays on the designer too, so the other pages are always one click away. The header bar carries only the title (no page name), and the column has no room count under it. The generator takes the floor, puzzle count and rooms-to-blood, and each floor fills its own real size like a Hypixel floor - Entrance 4x4 rooms, Floor 1 4x5, Floors 2-3 5x5, Floor 4 6x5, Floors 5-7 6x6 - as one compact rectangle, never scattered over the 6x6. **Load a Room** lists every room as a table - Name, Type, Size, Secrets, Crypts and Routes, each column as wide as its widest heading (sort arrow included) and cell so no two ever run together, Name taking the rest and cutting a long name with "..." (a narrow window drops Type, then Crypts, Size, Secrets and Routes as it must) - with a search box, a **Sort** button (Name, Type, Size, Secrets, Crypts, Routes; left click the next, right click the previous; counts sort biggest first) and column headings you can click to sort by that column, again to turn the order round; the sort is kept across restarts. Under the search are the Size chips (1x1, 1x2, 1x3, 1x4, 2x2, L) and the Secrets and Kind settings, the same filter as its Filters button (see Dungeon Sim Room Filters). A chosen chip is lit - filled in the theme's accent with bright text - and one that is not is dark with dim text; Secrets and Kind are lit when they are not on Any, and the Filters button is lit while any filter is on, its count being the number of chips chosen (two Size chips read "Filters (2)"). Sorted by Routes, the rooms with "-" (no route possible) come first, then "no routes", then rooms with route nodes by their count; the other direction turns all of it round. A room loads only when you click on its row - a click beside the list does nothing. **`/reset`** (or **`/reload`**) rebuilds the room you loaded on its own (from Load a Room, Change Room or All Rooms) exactly as it was loaded - every block, so a blown or broken wall is back, the secrets (chests shut, items, bats, essences, levers), the puzzle, the mobs and the doors - and puts you back where the room first put you, facing the same way; the map, Secret Waypoints' found marks and Auto Routes start over as on a fresh load, and chat says "Room reset". On a whole floor it resets nothing and says it is for solo rooms. Like the other sim commands it only exists inside the sim: anywhere else it is not suggested or completed, and a server's own `/reset` or `/reload` runs untouched. **Previous Runs** shows every saved run that has a map as a card: a small picture of the run's dungeon map beside the floor, score, time, date, room count, party and secrets, crypts and deaths, sorted by Newest, Oldest, Best Score, Fastest or Floor and filtered by Floor (both kept across restarts). Clicking a card opens the run's own page: the map large on the left and beside it everything the run recorded - time, score, the phases and device times, then the party (each player's class, rooms, secrets and deaths), which rooms each of them cleared alone and with others, and what killed them - with **Load This Run** to rebuild it and **< Runs** to go back. A rebuilt run puts every room down facing the way it did in the real run, so its doors meet: since 2026-10-15 a saved run records each room's rotation as the live map read it; a run saved before that, or a room whose rotation was never read, is turned to whichever way its doorways meet the doors on the run's map. Inside: etherwarp, Wither Impact, Tactical Insertion, Spirit Sceptre, Superboom, Dungeon Breaker and the Terminator (three arrows, and Salvation piercing five after three hits), plus Bonzo's Staff, the Jerry-chine Gun and Spring Boots, which launch him as Hypixel's server does (he takes no other knockback in the sim), all reimplemented locally, all swinging at Hypixel's 100 Attack Speed baseline. A refused ability says only what Hypixel says: "You cannot use abilities in this room!" in a trap room (Superboom and ender pearls still work there) or the Teleport Maze (only Superboom), "You cannot do that in this room!" for an etherwarp in Boulder, and "You don't have enough charges to break this block right now!" for an empty Dungeon Breaker (once a second while you keep trying, as on Hypixel); an etherwarp with no target, a Superboom aimed at nothing, a Dungeon Breaker in a puzzle room and Salvation's ready and hit counts say nothing in chat (each is in the log). You always have the Magnetic Talisman: items are picked up from three times vanilla's reach. One Superboom opens every crypt, crypt wall and prince with any block within 3 blocks of where it lands (measured to the block, not its center), each with its own undead, and takes the whole of each: a prince goes with its entire tomb, the player head on it and the layer under it (Big Red Flag's tomb is a prince too), and a cracked brick in reach takes its whole connected chain of cracked and chiseled brick, however far it runs. Ordinary stone brick beside them stays. Secret bats appear once you have been within 8 blocks of them for 5 ticks (a quarter of a second), and count for the room's secret total when killed. The lever-and-door rooms (Mines, Pressure Plates, Bridges, Raccoon, Logs, Arrow Trap, Redstone Warrior, Hallway, Knight, Atlas, Long Hall, Deathmite, Doors, Buttons, Dome, Cobble Wall Pillar, Golden Oasis) drop their door or gate when you flick the lever; the levers missing from the captures (Logs, Arrow Trap, Cobble Wall Pillar, Golden Oasis) are put back. The Redstone Key works as on Hypixel: the key is the real key skull, clicking it takes it (that is not a secret), and clicking the room's redstone block afterward puts the key in, which counts one secret for the room; in Golden Oasis that also drops the three redstone blocks by the lever. The Dungeon Breaker holds 20 charges, spends one per block and regains two a second, and every secret you get (chest, item, bat, essence) gives charges back, up to the 20: how many is the **Echoes of the Lost** slider in the mod menu's **Sim Settings** tab (the Wither Essence Shop perk of that name, whose five levels restore 1 to 5 charges per secret on hypixelskyblock.minecraft.wiki; 0 is the perk off, and it defaults to 5, the maxed perk) and it is saved across restarts. Mage is the only class, with the left-click beam, which has ZERO pierce - it kills the nearest thing you are looking at and nothing else. Mobs have 1 HP and never move, so a route is timed against the route rather than against where a mob wandered; starred ones carry the same name-tag stand Hypixel gives them, so Mob ESP and everything like it works unchanged. Fels sit as black skulls on the ground that nothing can hit or click, and wake into an upside-down enderman (named Dinnerbone, its name tag hidden) when you come within 9 blocks. Wither doors take a key, become barriers, then clear. **Spawn Starred Mobs** (an on/off switch in the mod menu's **Sim Settings** tab, the same saved setting as the Map Designer's **Mobs** button, Off by default and remembered; `/simmobs on|off` in the sim; since 2026-11-01) builds the next floor WITH MOBS: at GO two starred mobs a tile stand in every mob room - or, in a room whose spawn spots Blood Rush Helper has learned from real runs (since 2026-11-03), one starred mob on each learned spot, turned with the room's rotation (a spot the sim's copy of the room has no floor at, within two blocks up or down, is left out; a room with none learned keeps the seeded spots) (a miniboss in a miniboss room), and in about two mob rooms of five a starred Fel lies a few blocks from one of them (its star tag is there from the start, over a sleeping enderman nothing can hurt until it wakes), with an unstarred Fel skull in about one room of three; a room clears only when its last starred mob dies. The path from the Entrance to Blood gets its wither doors and the Blood door: each door after the first is a shut coal door whose key comes from killing the mobs of the mob room before it, the last mob room before Blood dropping the Blood Key instead. The key drops where the room's last starred mob died and is the team's once someone walks near it ("<name> has obtained Wither Key!"); it never goes in the inventory, and a right click on the door with no key does nothing. A key room's first entry by a teleport insta clears it and its key is lost, as on Hypixel. The Fairy door on the path is shut until GO and opens by itself 1.5 s later. A door whose rooms before it have no mobs to give a key stays open, so every floor can be finished. Such a floor also says Hypixel's lines at Hypixel's moments: "Starting in 1 second.", Mort's opening line at GO, "<name> opened a WITHER door!" and "The BLOOD DOOR has been opened!". Without Mobs, every mob room counts as cleared (map checkmark, score, Auto Clear) the moment you walk into it, without killing anything; puzzle rooms still clear by their puzzle: a solved puzzle turns its tab-list line to done and its room's map checkmark green, as on Hypixel (Ice Fill once you reach the end; Boulder the moment its chest is opened, whether or not the boxes were pushed). One mimic per map. The sim used to outline every chest that could have been it, and that outline is gone: because every placed secret chest is a mimic candidate, it amounted to highlighting every secret on the floor whether or not Secret Waypoints was switched on. The sim has no highlighter of its own now - Secret Waypoints is the only thing that draws secrets in there, under exactly the setting that draws them on Hypixel. Score follows Catacombs: one point per crypt to five (counted when the crypt's undead dies, as Hypixel's "Crypts" line does; a prince's death also says "A Prince falls. +1 Bonus Score"), two for the mimic (a trapped chest, as on Hypixel: opening it puts a baby zombie there, and the point comes when that dies; no chat line for either, as on Hypixel), Paul's EZPZ, and bats counting as secrets. `/speed <n>` sets speed on the Hypixel scale. `/simpuzzle <name>` builds a puzzle to drill. A full run starts by itself once the floor is built: a three-second countdown holds you in the entrance with the gate shut, then the gate opens and the clock starts (no /start command). **State, 2026-10-01:** secrets come from the room database and land inside their rooms (asserted by a scenario that builds a floor and checks every chest). A Catacombs sidebar is put up on the sim's own integrated server, so the floor, the secret count, the keys and the room you are standing in read as they do on Hypixel. Rooms are rotated now: each capture's own quarter turn is recovered from its roof marker and its secrets, so a rotated room still puts its secrets in the right corner. Braziers burn as they do on Hypixel (a capture that recorded one with its fire knocked out has it put back), and a doorway between two rooms keeps its own trim - the stairs, slabs or iron bars along the top of the opening - rather than being cut to a bare 3x4 hole. Rooms are joined only through their real doorways, which sit at the same height on every floor, as on Hypixel; a gap higher up a wall (Mage, Quad Lava) is never taken for a door and cut through. **The eleven puzzle rooms are bound to their real geometry rather than to invented arenas** - the room's own levers, buttons, pads, lanterns, ice and boulders, found by scoring each puzzle's coordinates against the pasted blocks at all four rotations and refusing to arm a room they are not in. That means your own puzzle solvers and auto-puzzles work in there against the same data they use on Hypixel; `docs/SIM.md` records what each one was measured against. **Not done:** mobs at their real spots in rooms Blood Rush Helper has not learned yet (the captures hold no mob positions, and the reference mod adm's spawn data is its own, All Rights Reserved, so those rooms place them on open floor by a seed); with Spawn Starred Mobs off a floor spawns no room mobs at all, and keeps only what the sim always spawns: secret bats, crypt undead and princes, the mimic's zombie and the puzzles' own entities; a handful of rooms have imperfect captures (Balcony, Catwalk, Purple Flags); and none of it is tuned against a real floor yet
- Dungeon Sim Teleports — **work in progress, sim only.** Two ways of getting around inside the dungeon sim, and neither command exists anywhere else: both are gated so that outside the sim they do not run, do not tab-complete and do not shadow whatever Hypixel does with those names. `/goto <room>` (also `/tpto <room>`) teleports you into a room by name, and the name tab-completes against the rooms on the floor you are standing in rather than against the whole 135-room library - so it can only ever offer you somewhere you can actually go. The argument is greedy, because room names have spaces in them ("Higher Blaze", "Lava Ravine"), and completion matches a prefix first and then any substring, so "blaze" offers both halves of the blaze pair; a name that matches two different rooms is refused rather than guessed. Where you land is the same place a room loaded on its own puts you (the picker, All Rooms, `/next`, `/back`): on the room's main floor, two blocks in from one of its doorways and facing into the room - the floor height most of its doorways share, on a solid block with nothing at your feet or head. On a generated floor a one-tile room uses the doorway a run walks in by. A few rooms keep the spot you asked for: the trap rooms, Teleport Maze and Boulder one block in from the entrance, Ice Fill two in, and the blaze rooms at their own level. `/c <blocks>` clips you that many blocks along the direction you are looking, and a negative number goes backward; it goes through whatever is in the way, which is the point of a clip in a practice world, and the height is clamped into the world so a clip straight down cannot drop you under the void. Both write your position directly, which this mod never does anywhere else and must never do on Hypixel - the sim's "server" is the client's own integrated server, no packet leaves the machine, and the same gate that guards every other sim ability (a singleplayer world AND no connected server) is what makes that true rather than hoped for In the F7 boss (killer560, 2026-11-09) `/tpto` and `/goto` take boss spots instead of rooms, and they tab-complete only there: `ss` (the Simon Says device, at the button), `s1` (the start of section 1), `hee2` (the high early-enter spot by the lever device), `ee3` (the low early-enter spot), `outcore` (just outside the core entrance), `storm` (the middle of Storm's arena), `maxor` (Maxor's arena start), `p5` (the dragon area) and `necron` (Necron's platform). The F7 Boss menu's Section 2, 3 and 4 buttons land on `hee2`, `ee3` and `outcore`, section 1 and Core are unchanged. Every weapon in the boss sim swings and shoots at 100% Attack Speed whatever your gear says, and a P5 dragon has one health pool of 15 bow hits, 5 Mage beams or any mix (an arrow is 1/15, a beam 1/5).
- Dungeon Sim Room Filters — **work in progress, sim only.** One Filters panel, the same everywhere the sim picks rooms: the Map Designer, Load a Room (also what Change Room opens) and All Rooms each have a **Filters** button that opens it on their own filter, so the three never look or behave differently, and each is saved in its own file so it survives a restart (`killer560smod-sim-designer-filters.json`, `-picker-filters.json`, `-allrooms-filters.json`). It is drawn in the mod's own style, like Design a Map: a black header bar titled FILTERS with "N of M rooms" on the right (amber once anything is filtered), one row per category with its label on the left and the mod's buttons as chips on the right, a chosen chip in amber with an amber border, and **Clear all** and **Done** at the bottom. **Size** (1x1, 1x2, 1x3, 1x4, 2x2, L shape) and **Kind** (Normal, Puzzle, Trap, Champion, Blood, Entrance, Fairy) choose any number - nothing chosen means any; **Rare room** (Any / Yes / No), **Secrets** (Any / None / 1 to 3 / 4 or more) and **Your routes** (Any / Has routes / No routes) pick one; **Puzzles** (each puzzle room by name) and **Crypts** (Any / None / 1 to 3 / 4 or more) are the designer's older filters kept in the same style. Everything comes from the room database: Kind is its room type (a rare room counts as Normal, Old and New Trap as Trap), Rare room is its rare type, Champion its four miniboss rooms, Fairy the fairy room. "Has routes" means at least one node in your Auto Routes file for that room - the same file and the same room names Auto Routes plays from, and one route covers every rotation - and "No routes" lists only rooms worth routing (not puzzles, Blood, Entrance, Fairy or 0-secret rooms). Chips wrap onto more lines when the window is narrow, the labels go above their chips when it is narrower still, and the rows scroll when they do not fit, so nothing ever overlaps. Every Load a Room row also shows its Auto Routes at a glance in its Routes column: a green node count when it has a route, a gray "no routes" when it has none. **Route Practice** on the Dungeon Sim menu is a page with three sets - **All Rooms**, **Rooms Without Routes** and **Rooms With Auto Routes**, each showing how many rooms it holds after the filters - and loads the first room of the one you pick, on its own. **`/next`** and **`/back`** then load the next or previous room of that set, one room at a time, with a chat line like "Room 3/41: Atlas (5 secrets, routes: no)". That is the only line a room load says (a room loaded by itself says "Built <room>"); the secrets placed and the lever doors opening are in the log. With no filter on, the sets leave out puzzles, Blood, Entrance, Fairy and 0-secret rooms, as they always did; choosing a Kind or a Secrets bucket replaces that part, so the filters can bring those rooms back (Kind: Puzzle steps through the puzzle rooms). The list is fixed when you pick the set, so a room you route while walking "Rooms Without Routes" keeps its place; pick the set again to refresh it. The walk stops at either end rather than wrapping - `/next` on the last room says so and loads nothing. While All Rooms is running the pause menu has **Next room (All Rooms)** under Change Room, and `/simbuild noroutes` jumps to the next room of the set with no route. **All of that only works in All Rooms**: on a room you loaded by itself (from Load a Room or Change Room), `/next` and `/back` load nothing and say "/next and /back only work in All Rooms", their keys do nothing, `/simbuild noroutes` says the same, and the pause menu has no next-room button. The Route Practice page has a **Keybinds** section with **Next Room** and **Previous Room** (unbound by default, a mouse button works too, Esc clears), which do the same as the commands; they are also a Keybinds section of the mod menu's **Sim Settings** card. Both commands and both keys only exist inside the sim, so on Hypixel `/next` and `/back` are untouched. (This replaced the old All Rooms preset, which pasted every captured room in one line, and the picker's Routes / shape / Puzzles buttons and the pause menu's "Next room with no routes" on a single room.)
- Map Designer — draw a floor by hand instead of generating one, on the Dungeon Sim menu's Create a New Map page (the page the menu opens on first in a game session; the menu's column of pages and its Back stay on the left). Under the grid and the room list are two rows: the floor, Rooms to blood, Puzzles and Generate, then Clear, Load, **Mobs** and Play, each a quarter of the row (Mobs: see Dungeon Sim). The Rooms to blood and Puzzles sliders stop at what the floor can really hold - a long path and many puzzles compete for a small floor's few cells - so Entrance goes up to 6 rooms to blood and its Puzzles top drops from 4 to 2 as Rooms to blood rises, Floor 1 drops from 5 to 2 puzzles between 5 and 8 rooms to blood, Floors 2-3 allow 4 puzzles at 8, and Floors 4-7 allow everything; changing the floor brings both values down to the new floor's tops, Puzzles follows Rooms to blood as you drag it, Random draws only inside those tops, and if a floor ever still comes out short the status line says so first. The grid on the left is the floor button's floor at its real size (Entrance 4x4 room slots up to 6x6 on Floors 5-7, scaled to fill the same square), with the floor and its size written above it; changing the floor button changes the grid, and when a floor is already generated it generates a new one of the new size at once (rooms you placed that no longer fit are taken off, and the status line says how many); the list on the right is every room in the library that can actually be pasted, searchable, with its kind, shape and secret count, and colored by room type the way the live map colors it. Click a room then a cell to place it, right-click a cell to take it out. The grid is drawn on whole screen pixels at every window size and GUI scale: evenly spaced lines, every cell the same size, and each placed room filling exactly its own cells (an L room its three cells) with the same small gutter inside the lines on every side, its name centered in it. Only the doors the build will really cut are drawn - a gap between two rooms that stays walled shows as a wall - and they are colored the way the dungeon map colors them: a narrow opening for an ordinary doorway, the blood color for the blood door, the entrance color for the entrance gate, and the wither color with an amber outline for a wither door. The sim builds no wither doors, so the doors a real Hypixel floor would have as wither doors - every door on the room path from the entrance to blood, except the entrance gate and the blood door - are drawn that way too, here and on the sim's live map (with a single outline there, and "A wither door on a real floor" in the interactive map's tooltip); never on a real Hypixel map. Room names are fitted to the room exactly as the dungeon map fits them, one word a line and scaled down until they fit, so a 1x1 shows its whole name rather than a truncated one. Every generated floor, from Generate here or from the random generator, holds one trap room (Old Trap or New Trap). A room whose footprint would run off the grid or over a neighbor is refused rather than squeezed in, so a drawing always builds the floor it shows. There is no Save: every floor you Play from here goes into the **Load** list with the date and time it was made, newest first, marked Generated or Drawn ("Floor 1 · Generated", "Entrance · Drawn"), with its room and puzzle counts (right-click a row to delete it), and clicking one puts that exact floor back on the grid at its own floor size, ready to play again - a generated floor comes back with the same rooms, rotations and doors. Playing a floor that is already listed does not list it twice. Floors from real runs are not in it (they are in the saved-runs screen). The list survives a restart (`killer560smod-sim-map-history.json`, kept out of profiles as history); a drawn floor naming a room you no longer have loads the rest and says how many it dropped. Each list row has a little space between its two lines. Designs kept by the old Save button (`killer560smod-simmaps.json`) were brought into the list once, as "Name · Saved design" dated by when that file was last changed; the old file is left as it was, and deleting one from the list keeps it deleted. Play hands the placements to exactly the code path the random generator ends in, so a floor you drew and a floor the mod invented are built identically and differ only in which rooms went where. The Filters button beside the search box opens the sim's Filters panel on the designer's own filter (see Dungeon Sim Room Filters for the rows). Here size, rare room, secrets, crypts and routes do not apply to puzzle rooms, which follow only the Kind and Puzzles rows. The filters narrow the room list and Generate. Generate always keeps Entrance, Blood, Fairy and a trap, and any room you pinned on the grid, whatever the filters say, so a filter can narrow a floor but never break it: if the rooms left cannot make a whole floor (no Normal rooms, say) it generates from every room instead and says so in chat and on the status line ("filters too strict"); if fewer puzzles are allowed than the Puzzles slider asks for, the floor gets the ones allowed and the status line says how many ("only 1 puzzle allowed (slider 3)"). The Filters button is lit while any filter is on and shows how many chips are chosen, Clear all clears them, and they are saved in `killer560smod-sim-designer-filters.json` so they survive closing the screen and a restart (filters saved by the first, slider-based version of this panel are not carried over). The room labels on the grid follow the live dungeon map's Room Labels setting and are drawn with the same fitting code and text scale: Secrets writes each room's secret count, Room Name writes the name, and Room Name + Secrets writes the count under the name, in the way the live map writes a room nobody has opened ("0/6", or a bare "0" for a room with no secrets, from the room database), in the unopened-room gray. Names are split one word a line and scaled down to fit small rooms. Off and Checkmarks have no text to draw, so the designer shows names for them. Entrance, Fairy and Blood keep their names here, which the live map omits
- Dungeon Sim Blood Camp — **work in progress, sim only.** (simblood-1110, killer560 2026-11-10; **Watcher Blood Camp** in the mod menu's **Sim Settings** tab, Off by default and remembered.) The Watcher runs the floor's blood camp in the sim's Blood room: the first player into Blood starts it, he appears over the middle of the room and says his greeting, a mob skull sits in a wall display case for each mob to come (9 plus the floor number: 16 on Floor 7), and before each one leaves he says one of his summon lines. The first wave of four leaves its cases between 7.5 and 12 seconds after the greeting and flies 16.1 blocks in a straight line over about 4 seconds; after "Let's see how you can handle this." (15 s) the rest follow one about every 2.7 seconds, each 11.9 blocks in about 2 seconds, the last one with "That will be enough for now.". Each skull lands as a one-HP blood mob (a zombie in that skull with a "Name 1❤" tag, named from the Watcher's undead), which nothing can hurt until the floor's move time (the Blood Camp helper's own per-floor table, 21-30 s after the greeting); he reacts to kills now and then, and 2 seconds after the last kill says "You have proven yourself. You may pass." and the boss portal opens in the middle of the room. Everyone in a co-op sim sees the same camp. The skulls fly the very path the Blood Camp helper predicts (start plus direction times 16.1 or 11.9), so its boxes, countdown, Trigger Bot and Blood Mobs ESP work on it unchanged. Off: no Watcher, and five seconds in Blood open the portal, as before. Timings the logs do not pin down are estimates (docs/SIM.md lists them); a real camp logs a "camp census" line to refine them.
- Dungeon Sim Real Mob Looks — **work in progress, sim only.** (simblood-1110; **Real Mob Looks** in the **Sim Settings** tab, shown while Spawn Starred Mobs is on, Off by default and remembered.) Spawn Starred Mobs puts its starred mobs on the spots Blood Rush Helper learned; with Real Mob Looks on, a spot whose real mob was seen on Hypixel spawns as that mob: the same entity type, armor and held items, player skin, star name and facing, turned with the room's rotation (a recorded miniboss name comes back as that miniboss). Spots never seen keep the plain dummy. The looks are recorded while you play: whenever Learn Spawn Spots confirms a starred mob standing on its spot on Hypixel (never in the sim), its look is saved for that room spot in the shared record store (`sim-mob-looks.json`, every instance on the PC; at most 48 a room). Only starred mobs ever spawn.
- Dungeon Sim F7 Boss — **work in progress, sim only.** The F7/M7 boss after the clear: once Blood is done (the Watcher's "You have proven yourself. You may pass.", or, until the sim's Blood room has a Watcher, five seconds standing in it) a portal opens in the middle of the Blood room and stepping into it starts the boss. The sim menu's F7 Boss entry (or `/simboss p1|p2|s1|s2|s3|s4|core|p4|p5|stop [floor7|master]` inside the sim) starts it straight at any phase; its Mode button picks Floor 7 or Master Mode (P5). Its Spawn Withers switch (default ON, saved) turns the boss withers off in every phase for a quiet arena to set up routes, waypoints and terminals in; the arena, terminals, devices, gates and lava still work, and a phase that waits for a boss kill simply waits. The room is built at Hypixel's own coordinates, from the capture of the real (reskinned) room that ships inside the mod, the same for every player and never replaced by a file on disk; only if that cannot be read does it fall back to a built-in plan that puts every terminal, lever, device, gate and platform exactly where Hypixel's are with plain floors and walls around them. Every boss moves and keeps time the way Hypixel's does since its October 2026 boss update. P1 Maxor: he flies at you from about four seconds in; click an Energy Crystal on its tower to pick it up (it sits in your last hotbar slot), place it at a pylon (they open four seconds in), and once both have charged the laser, bring him into the beam (stand on the strip just south of it): two laser hits kill him, and hitting him ends a stun sooner. P2 Storm: he flies his opening loop, waits, then chases you; a pad brings its pillar down a step at a time, and a pillar coming down with him under it crushes him, twice. P3 Goldor walks his loop round the sections (catching up when you outpace him) and flies into the core once everyone is in it: all 29 terminals, levers and devices (Simon Says, Lights, Arrow Align, the i4 target wall), the gates (they open five seconds after their section is done) and the core. The terminals are real menus with Hypixel's titles and layouts, so Terminal Solver, Auto Terminal and the auras work on them. P4 Necron runs Hypixel's own script: his lines, two trips out at you and one side platform turning to lava. On Master Mode, P5's five dragons fall to hits. Every line is Hypixel's own, said at Hypixel's pace, so the boss trackers (phases, P3 sections, Fast Leap, AP3) follow the fight. P3 and P4 lava bounces you up as Hypixel's does. The relics are not modeled.
- Dungeon Sim Multiplayer — **work in progress, sim only, not released.** Play the sim with friends. The sim menu has a **Multiplayer** entry, set apart above Back. **Create** names your room, sets an optional password, Max Players (a 2 to 5 slider) and **Start at** (the same boss phases as the single-player F7 Boss menu: P1, P2, P3 sections and core, P4, P5), starts the F7 boss sim there and opens it: the mod asks your router to open the port (UPnP) and says plainly when it could not ("friends on your network can still join; others need port forwarding of TCP port N"), and lists the room in a public room list. Only the room's name, owner, player count and address go through the mod's relay; the game itself is a direct connection to you, and when a friend cannot reach you directly the mod first tries a TCP hole punch (the two games connect to each other at the same moment, no router setup), and only if that fails does the game go through the mod's relay, so no room is ever LAN only. Routers without UPnP are also asked through NAT-PMP or PCP, and the opened port is kept for an hour at a time and renewed while you host. The relay has a free daily limit; once it is used up a friend who can only come through the relay sees "relay full today, host needs UPnP or a port forward". Each room in the list shows how a friend will most likely get in: [Direct], [Punch] or [Relay]. Each room in the list shows its Minecraft version; a room on another version is marked and Join says "Host is on 26.2 - open 26.2 to join." Every host builds the same reskinned F7 arena (it ships inside the mod). **Join** shows that list (fetched when you open it, on Refresh and at most every 30 seconds), asks for the password of a locked room, and also joins by address or from the LAN list. A player who did not come through the list (a ticket the relay hands out) is removed after about 12 seconds, and nobody gets in past Max Players. A player who joins, at any time, lands on a clear spot beside the owner (never the roof above the sim). The **Session** page lists who is in, with Kick for the owner, a **Move everyone to** picker and Move button (the owner only; the running fight restarts at that part and every player is placed on its spawn) and Leave for a guest (a guest opens it from the pause menu's "Sim Session"); the owner's switches decide what guests may ask for: Teleport (to the owner or a room), Speed (100 to 500), Fly, Apply My Loadout and Item, each done for that guest alone and answered in their chat. Closing the room or leaving the sim while others are in asks first ("1 player will be disconnected."), and a guest then sees "Session ended: the owner left" with a Back to Sim Menu button. **Invites:** the owner's Session page has **Invite Players**, which lists his friends, Best Friends and mod users who can take an invite right now, each with an Invite button, plus an "Invite by name" box. The invited player gets a message with Join, Decline and Block (a prompt on the title screen and sim menus; live invites are also at the top of the Join page), and Join takes them straight in with no password. Sim Settings' **Receive Sim Invites** (on by default) keeps the one quiet relay connection invites arrive through; off, nobody can invite you. Invites last 10 minutes and are rate limited. **Party chat:** in a session `/pc <message>` (also `/pchat`, `/party chat`) goes to everyone in the sim as "Party > Name: message", from the owner or a guest; alone it answers "You are not in a party right now." Every player's tab row reads the sim's one class, Mage.
- Map Logger — **dev builds only (cheat and legit; not in a release jar), always on, no setting.** Every dungeon room he stands in has its skull blocks written to `config/killer560/dungeons/maplog/killer560smod-maplog-<time>.log`, one line per skull with its position counted from the room's origin and its height in the capture's own heights, so the line can be compared with a room capture directly. Only skull blocks are read (decorative ones too, which is the point: etherwarp cannot land on them, so a sim room missing one sends the pathfinder through a wall). A skull is written once, the first time it is seen in a room; one that disappears later is written as "gone" and never added to the room, which is how a skull the Dungeon Breaker or Breaker Aura broke stays out of the record. A skull already broken when the room is first seen is never written. Chat stays silent; the log is a file, so profiles leave it out.
- Auto Debuff — **cheat build only.** The M7 dragon debuff, on the dragon the split says is yours: prefire Last Breath at the spawn box, stop in time to get the Ice Spray Wand in hand, jump ~300ms out, spray on the spawn tick, then swap and hit it. Healer and Tank use Flay (Soul Whip or Flaming Flay, either tier) at its real 0.5s cooldown; Mage melees instead, preferring Dark Claymore, then Midas' Sword, then a Hyperion with Chimera, then one with Ultimate Wise, with its own Mage Melee After toggle. Separate on/off per class for Mage, Healer and Tank - Archer and Berserker are never touched and have no setting. Stops once the dragon leaves the killbox. M7 only, since F7 has no Phase 5. The jump and spray have no slack between them, so the lead is ping-aware by default. **Walking is not implemented yet**: it does not path to the split, does not chase the dragon for the melee classes, and does not walk back on a death - so today you position yourself and it does the timed sequence
- Auto Update — keeps the mod up to date by itself, in Home's Auto Update section. Its Auto Update switch turns it on or off (on by default; off, it never updates), and its Channel picks what it follows: Full Release (the default) follows the newest numbered release, Beta follows the rolling Latest Dev Build that is rebuilt on every change (newer, less tested). On launch it asks GitHub, in the background, for the channel's release; if that release was built from a different commit than the jar you are running, it downloads the jar for your Minecraft version and your variant (it never switches you between cheat and legit), checks its SHA-256 against the release's checksum file, and refuses anything that does not match or does not come from the mod's own GitHub releases. A download that stops sending gives up after 60 seconds with no data (and says so); a slow one that keeps moving is never cut off. A chat line then says the update is ready. Nothing changes while you play: when you close the game a small helper waits for Minecraft to exit, removes the old jar and puts the new one in your mods folder, so there is never a second copy of the mod there. The next launch shows an "Updated to" popup with the release notes. Check Now runs the check on demand. Beta builds are release builds (no developer tools, quiet logs). Testing builds never auto-update, and neither do instances set up by the developer's deploy script
- First-Launch Tour — once per instance, for new players and for everyone who updates to a version that has it, about half a second after the title screen appears, killer560 himself shows you around: Minecraft's own 3D player model in his skin (shipped in the jar, so it works offline), waving, pointing at what he talks about and walking between spots (facing the way he walks, and toward what he talks about when he stands; standing he is still), with a round speech bubble in the menu's theme that pops in, floats, types its text and has its tail coming from his mouth. Its buttons, the page dots and the "About 1 minute" box are drawn in the bubble's own rounded look; the answer chips inside the bubble form an even grid (each line spans the bubble's full width with equal gaps, its chips equal in width, the text centered). About a minute: welcome (with Skip Guide), how to open the mod (your real Open Menu key and /killer560), the menu itself where you pick a theme and a main menu style, Fancy, Simple or Vanilla Main Menu (Fancy and Simple are the same setting as Home > Menu Theme's Main Menu chip, and the tour says Fancy's full picture downloads once while a low-res preview shows; Vanilla is no custom title screen, so nothing downloads; nothing changes until you click, and a click is applied and saved at once), which version you have (the cheat build warns you if you didn't mean to get it; the legit build tells you where the cheat one is), whether to share ban logs (Auto Report Bans) and whether to send crash reports (Auto Crash Reports), whether to link your Discord (Discord Link: Get Link Code shows a one-time code and the /link command to type in killer560's Discord, or Skip; never a question that holds Next) and whether to keep Auto Update on (each a Yes / No question: Yes is the suggested answer, Next waits until you click one, and your answer sets the setting), the menus, then the Discord, the YouTube channel and how to report bugs. Next and Previous (none on the first scene) or the arrow keys move through it; Escape asks before skipping. Skipping the tour that opened by itself turns ON every question you had not answered (Auto Report Bans, Auto Crash Reports, Auto Update; answered ones keep their answer, and a skipped Replay Intro changes nothing). It shows once and only once: it is marked seen the moment it opens (quitting the game mid-tour does not bring it back), the mark is kept per instance (never in profiles or the shared stats), and it never opens on its own again. Home > Mod & HUD > Replay Intro shows it again.
- Online Count — always on in every build, with no setting (killer560, 2026-11-02). Every copy of the mod reports, once a minute while on Hypixel or on the title screen (shown as "Main Menu"), an anonymous session id and which island you are on, so /online in the Discord can show how many mod users are online and where. The session id is random, made when the game starts and never saved or tied to your account; the island is the tab list's Area line ("Hub", "Dwarven Mines"), "Catacombs F7" in a dungeon run, or "Hypixel (not Skyblock)". Leaving Hypixel sends "Offline" once. No name, UUID, coordinates, mod version or anything else is sent, and the relay keeps no IP: only each session's island and when it was last seen, in memory, for 3 minutes. A failed send is silent.
- Ban Reports — Auto Report Bans, in Home's Ban Reports section (after Auto Update), legit and cheat alike. OFF by default, and only you can turn it on (a profile never changes it). If you're ever banned on Hypixel (the temporary or permanent ban disconnect screen, on a connection you made to hypixel.net), it sends ONE report through the mod's relay to the admins' private channel in killer560's Discord: the features you had on and their settings, this session's mod log (server corrections included, newest 5,000 lines, kept in memory while the switch is on), the ban message (Ban ID hidden) and the mod version, variant and Minecraft version. It never sends tokens, passwords or session IDs, other players' names or chat (names and UUIDs become <player> / <uuid>), your IP, e-mail or API keys, or your home folder; secret-looking settings are redacted. Only that ban screen starts a report: never chat or another kind of kick. Each ban is reported once, at most one report every 10 minutes and three a day; a failed send is retried the next time the ban screen shows. A chat line says whether it was sent.
- Crash Reports — Auto Crash Reports, in Home's Crash Reports section (next to Ban Reports), legit and cheat alike. OFF by default, and only you can turn it on (a profile never changes it); the first-launch tour asks. When the game crashes, it sends ONE report through the mod's relay to the admins' private channel in killer560's Discord: the crash report (Minecraft's crash-reports file, or the JVM's hs_err_pid log for a crash below Java), the mod version, commit, variant and Minecraft version, your other mods and the features you had on. It sends as the game crashes when it can (Minecraft's own crash report, up to 15 seconds), and otherwise on the next start, for the newest crash file only. Each crash is sent once, never one from before you turned it on or over a week old, at most one every 5 minutes and five a day. Before it leaves it is scrubbed of tokens and keys (the launcher's access token too), your IP and server addresses, other players' names and UUIDs, e-mail addresses and your home folder; your own Minecraft name and UUID stay only while Include My Minecraft Name is on (ON by default, killer560 2026-11-05), and with it Include My Discord (ON by default, shown only while the name switch is on) lets the relay name the Discord you linked in Discord Link, without a ping; not linked, nothing is added. A chat line says whether it was sent.
- Discord Link — Home's Discord Link card, legit and cheat alike: links this Minecraft account to your Discord account so killer560 knows who you are (on your crash reports now, and other things later). Link Discord gets a one-time code from the mod's relay (it works once, for 10 minutes; a new code replaces the old one); type /link code:<code> in killer560's Discord (Copy /link Command puts it on the clipboard). The card turns to "Linked to <your Discord>" by itself once you did (it asks the relay every 8 seconds while the code lives), and Unlink deletes the link. Check Status asks again. The link is kept only on the relay, never in a file or a profile; the relay checks your Minecraft account the same way as Mod Chat. The first-launch tour asks too, after crash reports (Get Link Code or Skip; it never holds the tour up, and if the relay cannot be reached it says to try later from Home > Discord Link).
- Reboot — `/reboot` (the menu's Home page no longer has a Reboot button, since 2026-10-15) closes the game and starts it again once it has fully closed. It only works when Prism Launcher (or PolyMC / MultiMC) started the game: the instance is found from the game folder, and anything else (the official launcher, CurseForge, a dev run) gets a chat line saying it cannot reboot and nothing happens. Connected to a server it asks first: click the chat line or type `/reboot confirm` within 10 seconds. Singleplayer and the title screen reboot straight away. The game closes the normal way, so settings save and an Auto Update waiting to install goes in before the game starts again. A small helper process waits for the game to exit (at most 2 minutes, then it gives up and starts nothing) and then asks Prism to launch the same instance; it logs to `config/killer560/system/reboot/killer560smod-reboot/reboot.log`. To put it on a key, bind `/reboot` in Command Keybinds.
- Update Notice — tells you once, the first time you join a world each launch, if a newer release of the mod exists, with the version you are on and a link. It is a single check on startup, not constant background checking, and it stays silent if it cannot reach GitHub - so playing offline or behind a filter gets you nothing rather than an error you can't act on. Only full releases count; pre-releases and the Latest Dev Build are ignored. On by default (the one exception to new features shipping off, because the notice exists to catch people running a months-old jar), and turned off with Notify Me Of Updates on the Home tab, where Check For Updates still works on demand
- Screenshot Copy — automatically copies a screenshot to your clipboard the moment you take it
- No Fire — removes the on-screen fire overlay so burning doesn't block your view mid-fight
- Discord Rich Presence — shows "Playing Killer560's Mod" on your Discord profile, with your island/area, dungeon floor and boss phase, and an elapsed timer. Off by default; a Hide Details mode shows only the mod name
- Object Hider — client-side render suppressors for M7/F7: healer fairy, power orbs (with a keep-nearby radius), soulweaver skulls, archer bone meal, sheep, wither cloak creepers, the Witherborn wither a full F7 armor set spawns (and its smoke and swirl particles), shield hearts, grounded arrows, blindness, death animations, block-break/explosion/smoke particles, dying P5 dragons, the Wither King model, boss damage splashes, and a Clean End mode, and No Glow Outlines (hides the vanilla glow outline on every entity; the Mob ESP and Commission Mobs glow styles still draw). Nothing is removed from the world — it only stops rendering. The vanilla health/armor/hunger bar hides live under Health and Mana Bars, the chat-line hides under Chat Hider in the Chat category, and Hide Players has its own section. Its first switch turns the whole feature on or off (off by default; it is also the switch on its menu card): off, nothing in the pack hides anything, No Fire Overlay included
- Ability Cooldowns — automatic cooldown HUD: detects the ability you used (its sound, the mana action-bar line, or chat) and counts down the real cooldown from a 41-item table ported from SkyHanni, with a dungeon-only filter and a click window so a teammate's ability can't start your timer
- Goldor Frenzy Timer — a HUD countdown to Goldor's 3-second P3 damage tick, plus the Storm-to-Goldor gap and an optional cumulative P3 timer

**Dungeon**
- RNG Meter — tracks your Hypixel Skyblock RNG/pity progress on dungeon floors; an open RNG Meter page keeps being read while it is open, so rewards that arrive after the page appears are still picked up
- Chest Profit — shows the value of every reward, the cost, and the profit next to any open dungeon reward chest (end-of-run room or Croesus), and in a Croesus run view lists every chest's profit. In that chest view, behind the items: **Highlight Free Chest** fills the most profitable chest green (the free claim) while nothing of the run is claimed yet and that chest makes a profit, and **Highlight Key Chest** fills orange the one chest worth coming back for with a Dungeon Chest Key - the best of the rest whose profit after the key clears Auto Croesus's key settings. Only one orange ever; after a claim without a key there is no green and the same chest stays orange; after a key, nothing. A run shows as needing a key in the run list exactly when its chest view has an orange chest. Both colors are settings (green and orange by default); Highlight Free Chest takes over the old Highlight Best setting, and Highlight Key Chest replaces Second Best With Key. Uses the live Bazaar/AH prices with its own **Price Mode** (left click cycles, right click back): **Insta-Sell** (what selling instantly gets) or **Sell Offer** (the lowest sell offer, what a sell order gets); Auction House items always use the lowest BIN. The same mode drives the profit log and Auto Croesus's decisions. A config from before the setting starts where the RNG Meter's Use Instant-Buy Price left it (on = Sell Offer). On by default. The panel beside the chest is its own element in Edit HUD (Croesus Chest Profit): open Edit HUD over a Croesus menu or a reward chest and drag or scroll it there; until you do it sits beside the chest. Chest Profit, Price Mode and the logger are the Croesus tab; Auto Croesus has its own tab (cheat build), the way Experiments and Auto Experimentation Table are split. Also in the Croesus tab, each its own switch and off by default (2026-10-25): **Reroll Protection** blocks your click on a reward chest's Kismet *Reroll Chest* button while that chest's profit is over **Protect Above** (5M by default, up to 20M) - or while prices have not loaded, since the chest's worth is then unknown; nothing is sent, chat says why once per chest screen, and holding Ctrl lets the reroll through (a reroll the button already refuses - no Kismet Feather, or this run's reroll already used - is left alone). Auto Croesus's own rerolls are not affected. **Kismet Count** draws the Kismet Feathers in your inventory (not your sacks, which the client is never told) as the number on the Reroll Chest button, green, or a red 0. **Croesus Chests HUD** is a HUD element (Croesus Chests, movable in Edit HUD) showing Croesus's unclaimed chests out of 60 as Hypixel's own tab list states them ("Unclaimed chests: N", in the Dungeon Hub) and your Kismet Feathers; it shows where the tab has the line and in dungeons, where the last count is shown with its age; it never guesses a count the tab has not given. Once the count reaches **Chest Warning At** (55 by default, 1-60) chat warns once, and again for each further chest
  - *Highlight Runs* (in the Croesus menu): each run shows what is left in it, behind its item and under its tooltip: the runs that need you stand out and the rest recede. **Unclaimed** is a solid orange fill with a thick border; **worth a key** is a solid magenta fill, thick border and a small key mark in the corner (you claimed one chest and another one clears Auto Croesus's key settings, so go back with a Dungeon Chest Key). **Opened** (one chest claimed, nothing left worth a key) is a muted blue outline with no fill, a claimed run whose chests were not looked at yet a muted teal outline with no fill (nothing to do there until you open it once), and **key used** ("No more chests to open!") a light dark tint. Whether a run is worth a key comes from its chests as seen in its run view (you or Auto Croesus opening it), remembered across restarts. Testkit 898 measures that every state stays distinct over green and red run heads and for red-green color blindness, and that the two action states are far more salient than the three quiet ones. All five colors are settings; a config still on an old default moves to the new one, a color you picked stays. *Color Legend* (on by default) adds a one-line key to the info box. The info box counts **Unclaimed runs X / 60**, **Already claimed** and **Worth a key** across every Croesus page you have opened this session (Croesus keeps at most 60 runs, hypixelskyblock.minecraft.wiki); until every page has been seen the totals show a "+" and "Pages seen n of N". A page you have not reopened since claiming from it keeps its last count.
  - *Hide Opened Runs* (off by default): runs with nothing left to do (opened with nothing worth a key, or key used) are not drawn. Only the picture is gone: hovering still shows the run and clicking still opens it. A run worth a key, or not looked at yet, is never hidden.
- Dungeon Profit Tracker — `/croesus` opens the profit screen, rebuilt 2026-09-28 to the layout killer560 asked for. **Overview**: Net Profit, Gross Value and Chest Cost as headline cards, a Rare Drops list, and a net-per-run graph with Session/Overall and a floor filter, plus the most recent chest. **By Floor**: session and all-time totals per floor. **Loot Log**: every item, how many, which floors and which runs. It added no new tracking - the log already stored per-floor totals, a per-item index and every claim's cost, value and profit, which is exactly what the concept needed. The cards and the graph live in a shared place so the same screen can serve other skills later. On by default
- Auto Croesus — **cheat build only.** Its own tab, in the Dungeon category's cheat block. Press the Start Croesus button drawn over an open Croesus menu and it opens each unopened run and claims its single most profitable chest if it clears your Min Profit, with random Min/Max click delays; optionally spends a real Dungeon Chest Key on a second chest once it clears a threshold you choose (Chest Key Min Profit, 0 to 2,000,000 in 20k steps; the key settings sit right under Use Chest Keys, then Use Kismets and its settings) (before claiming the first chest it values the others with the key's price taken off; if one is worth a key, then after the claim closes Croesus it re-opens him, goes back into that same run and claims it) - a set profit number, or the key's own live Bazaar insta-buy or buy-offer price (Key Buy-Offer by default; falls back to the set number until that price loads) - and optionally spends a Kismet Feather to reroll a poor Bedrock chest; skips anything below the minimum, and stops on any unexpected screen, or when you press Escape or the Emergency Cancel Key (Home) while it runs; other keys are kept off the menu and do not stop it Kismet rerolls come first (2026-11-01): before any chest of a run is claimed, a Bedrock chest worth less than Reroll Below is rerolled with a Kismet Feather whatever else the run holds, and the run is judged again from the rerolled chest (claimed if it clears Min Profit, otherwise refused and the next best chest taken). Once per run, never a chest that needs a key, and only on the floors switched on under Reroll on floors (F1-F7 and M1-M7, all on by default; green is on).
- Leap Message — sends a message when you leap to someone in dungeons (the Leaping To line first, then the Cringe line if that is on, half a second apart so Hypixel takes both)
- Milestone Display — a small HUD "Milestone: N" counting the dungeon milestone lines Hypixel prints this run ("Milestone ❖: You have dealt ... so far!"), red at 0 and 1, orange at 2, green from 3, counting up to 9. Counts per run, hides nothing; move it in Edit HUD. Both builds, off by default (featF-1108; SkyHanni's milestone display).
- Salvage Helper — marks cheap dungeon gear (a lore line with DUNGEON but not DUNGEON ITEM, lowest BIN known and under Max Item Value: 50k, 100k, 250k, 1M or 5M) in the Salvage Items menu (yellow) and the Trades menu (green), over the menu and your inventory beside it. Highlight only, it never clicks; Auto Sell stays cheat-only. Both builds, off by default (featF-1108; Skyblocker's Salvage Helper).
- Leap Counter — counts the teammates who leap to you in F7/M7 Phase 3 (S1 to S4 Leaps, each 0 to turn it off), in the core (Core Leaps) and in Phase 5 (Relic Leaps), and alerts ("Everyone Leaped!" by default) once the expected number have arrived. A teammate counts when they cover the Jump Distance quickly and land within the Leap Radius of you. In the core any leap that lands anywhere inside the core counts while you are in it, not only one that lands near you; your own leap in the last 3 seconds only holds back teammates who simply came into view, so a real leap right after yours still counts. Every arrival it drops is logged once with the reason.
- Leap Order — opened by `/leaporder` (there is no separate Class Overrides tab any more - class picks are stored with the Leap Order, and an old Class Overrides file moves into it the first time it loads), or the Leap Menu tab (Open Custom Leap Order Editor). The first page is five tall class cards across the whole screen, each listing the party members on that class now; pick the class you're playing and everyone goes where the Default Order puts their class (Odin: Archer top-left, Berserk top-right, Healer bottom-left, Mage/Tank bottom-right; A-Z Class; Z-A Class; or Hypixel's own order), a "Show Leap Order Reset" switch in the Leap Menu tab (on by default) hides the "Leap Order was reset" title and chat line; moving the moment a class is set; click a name then a spot to move it by hand, and moved names stay put (marked "pinned") until you shift-click them or press Reset Positions; Reset Classes clears the class picks for the party, so the tab list's classes apply again; opening the editor refreshes the party list by itself (at most every 10 seconds, not in a dungeon); each class keeps its own layout, and the custom leap menu uses the one for the class you're playing: the class the dungeon tab list shows you on (it wins over a class pick on your own name), else the class Hypixel last said you selected ("You have selected the Tank Dungeon Class!", a Party Finder group, the dungeon's pre-start line; kept across restarts), else the class last opened in the editor; the lit class card on the first page is that class; after you pick your class, any teammate whose class isn't known yet starts a class wizard: one page per teammate with their face, what the tab list says (pre-filled), a Class button (left click forward, right click back) and the five classes, then Back / Skip / Next at the bottom, and the layout after the last one; class picks and pins last until a run starts without that player, who then goes back to their tab-list class and default spot, or until a run starts with that player on another class on the tab list (their pick and pins were for the old one, and a "Leap Order was reset" notice says so); a newcomer takes the spot of the teammate they replaced, where that teammate stood in the last run's layout (two newcomers fill the vacated spots in spot order, in leap-menu order), but only while that layout still holds: when someone who stayed changed class, or you play another class than last run, newcomers go where the Default Order puts them and the reset notice is shown; one out and one in with nothing else changed gives the newcomer the leaver's class too; the same happens when a teammate leaves and another joins during a run (once the tab list has shown the change twice), and the next run is compared with the party that finished the run; if more than two leave, every pick and pin resets; a Chat button in the top-right corner of every page (the class cards, the wizard and the layout) opens a chat panel down the left side, closed each time the editor opens: it shows your chat (newest at the bottom, the mouse wheel or Page Up/Down scrolls back, and it follows new lines while at the bottom) and has a box at the bottom where Enter sends the line as normal chat (a line starting with / runs as a command, Up/Down recall what you sent), with the editor staying open; opening the panel puts the cursor in the box, Escape leaves the box (a second Escape closes the editor), and the page shrinks to the space right of the panel while the bottom buttons keep their places. Nothing is read from chat into the class picks
- Auto Routes — **cheat build only, work in progress — not part of the current release.** Record a route through a dungeon room (`/ar start record`) and play it back when you etherwarp onto its start node. Recording captures your actual path; playback follows it rather than replaying key presses blind, so a lag spike doesn't desync the rest of the route, and every etherwarp / item use / block break waits for real confirmation before moving on. `/ar add` takes exactly ten types - `boom`, `breaker` (or `dungeonbreaker`), `ew` (or `etherwarp`), `use`, `walk`, `path`, `crypt`, `bat`, `jump <number>` and `align w<width> l<length>` - and after the type only `start` and `await:<number>` (wait for that many secrets first - or, on a crypt or bat node, kills; a bare `await` or `await:` waits for 1; `await:x` is refused, and so is anything else). In a trap room (New Trap, Old Trap) any type also takes a box, `w<n> l<n>` as an align does (`/ar add breaker w3 l3`): its trigger is that W x L box instead of the ring, kept in the routes file and editable in the node editor; outside a trap room a box is refused and nothing is added. A walk node in a trap room turns your body onto its direction before it walks, as an AP3 walk does (the camera is held where you had it until the walk ends), instead of easing the camera round while it already moves. While a route runs, Secret Aura clicks secrets only on a node waiting for them (killer560, 2026-10-16): a chest, lever or skull you pass mid-route is left alone, so a route that needs one taken needs an `await` node in reach of it; items you walk over are still picked up. A node goes on the center of the block you stand in - whole blocks, not AP3's half-block grid - with the height kept at your feet; recording places its nodes the same way. Nodes saved on the old half-block grid keep working where they are, and land on a block center the next time the editor moves them. A node you add fires straight away, as if you had just walked onto it: `/ar add ew` warps you the moment the chat closes (the first tick no screen is open), the rest of the route carries on from it as it would from any node - including the landing re-fire, so adding the second of two etherwarps aimed at each other starts the bounce - and it fires even with Start From Start Node Only on. If you step off it before the chat closes it does not fire, and while a route is already running it only latches as before. It is still one `/ar undo` step. On Hypixel it sends exactly what walking onto the node sends. A `start` node goes off the moment you step onto it, even mid-stride with W held - keys you were already holding are overridden while a node runs and only a fresh press counts as taking over - and it is the node the Interactive Map warps you to. Start nodes draw filled in, in every render style. On a route built with `/ar add` a walk node sprints the way you were facing when you placed it, until the next node fires, you take the keys or the mouse back, or you run into something. Mouse clicks never stop a route. A right click never skips an await (it is how you click the chest the await waits for), but a **left click while a node waits on its await fires the node now** - the etherwarp goes even though the secret has not been got. That left click is taken by the route: no swing and no block hit goes out with it, so the warp is the only thing it sends, and it is only taken while a node is actually waiting, or while the route is warping you onto the next node (anywhere else a left click is a normal left click). When an await is met by a chest, lever or skull click (yours or Secret Aura's), the etherwarp goes the next tick, already facing its block: Hypixel's server takes an item use right after a click on a block as that click's second half when the look it last heard (the chest's) points at a block, and then nothing happens - so after a click every automatic item use lets the server hear its own look first (the body turns that tick, the camera stays), and when its target is within reach it sends the click on that block first, as your own right click does. Nothing waits on a timer: Auto Routes' warps, use nodes and Kill Mimic, the Interactive Map's hops (so Auto Clear, Auto Secret, the Dungeon Autopilot and Blood Rush trips too) and Auto Puzzles' shots and warps turn first only when it matters, and AP3's plain use, Fast Leap, Auto Fairy Souls' pearl, Mask Swapper's rod, Auto I4 and the Goldor Triggerbot's shots send what your own right click would. A left click made while the warp or path onto a waiting node is still in flight is held for half a second and skips that node's await as it arrives, and a left click while Kill Mimic is using its item for the waiting node skips it too (Kill Mimic stops). It skips whatever the node waits for - secrets, a secret bat, or a crypt node's wait for its crypt/prince kill (the node lets go of the held click and the route moves on) - whatever is under your crosshair. Only your own mouse click counts: attacks and digs the mod makes itself (Breaker Aura, Secret Aura, Auto Clear, the route's own nodes) never skip anything. Tab completion offers `await:` on its own, so you type the number straight after it. Command nodes in a routes file always run (the Allow Command Nodes switch is gone). Each `/ar` command has an assignable keybind; Start and Stop Recording have theirs right under their buttons, the rest are in a Keybinds dropdown. The settings tab runs: the Auto Routes switch (full width), Start From Start Node Only (full width), Run While Map Open beside Kill Mimic, Mimic Timeout under Kill Mimic, Crypt Weapon beside Crypt Attack Time, Etherwarps Per Second (full width), Open Routes Folder beside Reload Routes, then Render Style (style, node numbers, thickness, height, Breaker Block Display and Breaker Block Style), Recording (Start / Stop with their keys under them), and the Colors and Keybinds dropdowns last, Colors directly above Keybinds - every row in even half-width columns, as AP3's tab is. The per-room node list is gone from the menu; `/ar list`, `/ar delete`, `/ar undo` and `/ar clear` do all of it. In the Filled render style each node is drawn one block lower, inside the block it was placed on top of (only the drawing moves - where it triggers is unchanged; Box and Cylinder draw where they always did). In the Cylinder style a start node's fill is a cylinder between its two rings, not a box poking out past them. `/ar edit db` (or the short `/ar db` / `/ar editdb`) still picks a breaker's blocks, and right after `/ar add breaker` it picks them for the breaker you just added, wherever you stand; otherwise for the nearest breaker. While breaker edit mode is on (`/ar edit db`, `/ar db`, or Pick Blocks in the node editor) and you hold your Dungeon Breaker, a left click is a normal left click - you can break blocks while picking. Right click adds the block you look at and shift-right-click removes it, as before; picking sends nothing of its own - only your own left clicks break blocks. While edit mode is on no route runs and no breaker node fires, even one you stand in. Any block your crosshair can pick is accepted: the reach is measured from your eyes to the block (4.5 blocks, the Dungeon Breaker's normal dig reach), not from the node, and if the block is further than that from the node's own spot the chat line says so, since the node breaks from wherever the route has you when it fires. Edit mode only answers to the Dungeon Breaker in your hand: with your AOTV, Hyperion or anything else held, both mouse buttons work normally in edit mode, so you can etherwarp, use abilities and hit things while picking, and no block is picked. **Breaker Block Display** (Highlight / Waypoint, in Render Style) chooses how those picked blocks are drawn in edit mode - Highlight like any block outline, hidden behind walls; Waypoint through walls anywhere within your render distance - and **Breaker Block Style** makes them Outline, Fill or Filled Outline (the default, the old look); still-standing blocks are white and already-broken ones red.
