Its first row is **Theme**: Default (the theme the mod menu is on, and what new installs start with), then Amber, Dark, Light, Ocean and Viridian, any theme you saved, Vanilla (vanilla's own light gray, with its darker hotbar and selection ring) and Custom. On Custom a **Customize** row opens a menu with the Custom look's Base (Amber, Dark or Light), Slot Color Source / Slot Color and Accent Source / Accent Color; those overrides apply to Custom only. A file from before this carries its old preset over (Default became Vanilla, Dark became the menu's Dark, Mod Theme became Amber). Items, Item Rarity Backgrounds, the hover highlight, Inventory Search and Item Protection stars sit exactly on the slots, as in vanilla; the hotbar keeps its bottom edge on windows whose height is not a multiple of the GUI scale. Everything it draws is opaque. The selected hotbar slot gets vanilla's own ring in the theme's accent (a muted gray on Dark). Also: Applies To (Hypixel/p3sim menus only, or every container), and Theme Hotbar with the Hotbar Scale slider. The potion effects panel beside the inventory is not part of the theme: it is Hide Potion Effects, a card of its own.
- Hide Potion Effects — hides the potion effects panel vanilla draws beside your inventory (and the creative inventory): Absorption, Haste, Strength and the rest. Off by default, with the Inventory Theme on or off; turn it off to see the panel again. Chests, the Auction House and the Storage Overlay never draw that panel. A second switch, **Hide Effect Icons (HUD)**, also hides the effect icons in the top right of the screen while you play (off by default). The card's switch stands for both. A "hidePotionEffects" value saved in the Inventory Theme's settings before 2026-10-18 is not read, so it cannot bring the panel back.
- Mod conflict warnings — one chat warning per session if Skyblocker's terminal solver or Devonian's Party Finder Overview is doing the same job as ours
- Item Browser — a NEU-style full-height panel listing the complete Skyblock item catalog with real icons beside any inventory screen (only on inventory screens by default; the Only In Inventory toggle turns that off); adjustable column count (3-20), scale, horizontal/vertical fill order and left/center/right anchoring, hovering an item shows its real tooltip (lore from the NEU item repo, with its value), and a left-click craft and obtain popup with recipes from the NEU item repo (crafting grid with counts, forge, NPC shop and other ways to get it). Every minion is one stacked cell; clicking it opens a popup of all its tiers, and clicking a tier shows that tier's recipe. Sorts by value high to low by default (also low to high, A-Z, Z-A). It has no search box of its own: it filters by the Inventory Search bar at the bottom middle of the screen (the panel stops above it when it reaches over it), so keep Inventory Search on to use them together
- Item Rarity Backgrounds — colors the slot behind every Skyblock item by its rarity (Common through Divine/Special/Ultimate, pets included) in inventories and optionally the hotbar, with Square/Circle/Outline styles (adjustable outline width) and adjustable opacity (visual only). The same box, sized to the cell, also sits behind items in the mod's own item grids: the Inventory Sorter's layouts menu, Custom Items, the Storage Overlay, the Auction House and Bazaar inventory panels, the Item Browser and the Pet Wheel
- Slot Text — small text in the corner of items in menus and your inventory, read from the item and the open menu: pet level (top left), enchanted book level (bottom left) and an optional short enchantment tag (top left, ultimates in purple; the short names other Skyblock mods use: TL for Thunderlord, SH Sharpness, GK Giant Killer, UW Ultimate Wise, SE Soul Eater), minion tier (top right), potion level and Attribute Shard level (bottom right), Rancher's Boots speed cap (bottom left), skill levels in Your Skills (bottom left, gold when maxed), collection tiers (bottom right), Bestiary and Fishing levels (bottom right, gold at 100%), Heart of the Mountain and Heart of the Forest perk levels, and the points in Stats Tuning. Each kind has its own switch. Show is Always, Hold Key to Show or Hold Key to Hide (default key Left Alt, a mouse button works too); Skyblock Only is on by default. Container slots only, not the hotbar; visual only, off until switched on
- Compactor Preview — hover a Personal Compactor or Personal Deletor (4000-7000) and its tooltip gets a grid of the items you set up in it (read from the item's own saved slots; 1, 3, 7 or 2x6 slots by tier, an empty one just says how many slots it has, a deletor also shows Active YES/NO), so you do not have to open it. Show is On Hover or While Holding Key (default Left Alt; a mouse button works too). Item icons come from the Item Browser's catalog when it is on, otherwise a look-alike vanilla item (enchanted ones glint). Visual only, off until switched on
- Gyrokinetic Wand Overlay — (to-do Doc, 2026-11-01; Skyblocker's Gyro Overlay; off by default) while you hold the Gyrokinetic Wand in your main hand, draws the 10-block ring its ability pulls mobs from, centered on the block or mob you are looking at up to 24 blocks away (nothing when you look at the sky). Style: Circle Outline (the default, with a Line Width), Circle (a filled disc) or Sphere; Gyro Color sets the color and how see-through it is (half see-through purple by default). Visual only: it sends nothing. In Items
- Storage Item Search — search every cached ender chest page, backpack, wardrobe page, Equipment Wardrobe page (/equipment), pet page, island chest and your inventory by name, id or lore; enchanted books are listed by their enchant. Click a result to open that storage with the item outlined, or - for island chests - to box every chest holding that item. A Scan All button opens every storage, wardrobe and pet page once so all of them are remembered; it reads Hypixel's Storage menu first and never opens a locked Ender Chest page or an empty backpack slot
- Waypoint Routes — per-area waypoint routes that advance as you reach each point, with keybinds to add/remove/skip, and clipboard import/export in ColeWeight and Skytils formats (an import keeps at most 64 routes of 2000 points each, with names and labels cut to 48 characters, and says so in chat when it cuts)
- Custom Mage Beam — replaces the mage beam particles with a clean colored beam (color, real thickness up to a full block, duration, fade, and a Shape: Square, the default, a square beam coming out; Flat, a horizontal ribbon; or Cylinder - the beam is fixed in the world, so it does not turn as you run round it; and a Color Mode: Solid, Fade from the beam color at its start to a Fade Color at its end, or Rotating, a rainbow along the beam that turns over time). The beam is one even color across its width: the thin core line that keeps a very thin or far beam visible is drawn only while the body is under 2 pixels wide on screen
- Auto Quiz / Auto Three Weirdos — **cheat build only.** Clicks the solver's answer in Quiz and opens the right chest in Three Weirdos. Walking into the Quiz room is what starts it on Hypixel (no click on Oruo); Auto Quiz presses the answer's button on the first tick Oruo's cooldown allows - 220 server ticks after his first line, 100 after "2 questions left..." and "One more question!" (NoammAddons' and Odin's timings, matched by his own Hypixel logs); an earlier press does not count. With Talk To NPCs on, Auto Three Weirdos talks to each weirdo itself, one a tick, within the 3-block entity reach; when the ones left are out of reach it etherwarps (Etherwarp Reposition, an AOTV in the hotbar) or walks (auto-puzzle pathing, the Interactive Map when no walk or warp line gets there) onto the spot that has as many of them, and their chests, in reach as it can, and does the same for the chest if it ends up out of reach. Chest Open Delay defaults to 0 (the chest is clicked the tick it is known and in reach); a config still at the old 250 ms default is moved to 0 once
- Auto Puzzles (QUOI port) — **cheat build only.** An auto for every other puzzle, each needing its solver on: Auto Blaze, Auto Creeper Beams and Auto Ice Path shoot your shortbow (shared Shoot cooldown), Auto Boulder starts when you walk into the room or any teleport (an Interactive Map trip or your own etherwarp) lands you on its doorway spot, and it sprints on every walk: with Secret Aura on and its Chests option on it runs straight along the barrier roof to the iron bars at the back and lets the aura take the chest, pressing nothing; otherwise it walks down the stairs (from the map's doorway spot it first steps inside the room, because the Boulder Solver only reads the boxes from inside), presses only the buttons the Boulder Solver names and opens the chest by looking at it, turning like a mouse would and drawn smoothly every frame, and either way it then gets you back out of the room so you can etherwarp again: once nothing but open floor at your height lies between you and the doorway (along the roof from the bars, or from the top of the stairs) it aims at a point just past the doorway and sprints straight there, so it runs in and out without stopping; its other walks slow for corners and stairs by letting go of forward in time, so they keep their line at any speed (your sim's speed 600 included), and it walks on to the next button as soon as the box the last press pushed has moved. Auto Water Board / Auto Tic Tac Toe click the right levers and cells (Auto Tic Tac Toe first etherwarps onto the block right in front of the board from which every square is in reach, and with its **Grab Secret** option on it etherwarps right after the first click onto a block within Secret Aura's reach of the room's secret chest (walls do not matter - the aura clicks through them), lets Secret Aura take it - or opens it itself if the aura will not - and etherwarps back in front of the board), Auto Teleport Maze walks to the right pad - the exit pad once the solver has narrowed it down, by the shortest way over the pads it has already learned; otherwise it tries a new pad, and when its chamber has none left it goes back over known pads to the nearest chamber that does, so it never wanders back through the maze (round the walls in the middle of a chamber's sides rather than into them, and onto the start pad by itself when the Interactive Map has just brought you to the room), Auto Ice Fill steps the path with your AOTV (on each section's first tile it always waits for the server to register the tile before hopping on, adaptive or not; Adaptive mode waits like that on every tile, so lag can't make it fail), and if a section breaks under you it stops everything, waits until that section has come back as fresh ice (Hypixel brings back only the broken one, about two seconds later; it gives up with a chat message after 15 s), then etherwarps you back onto that section's first tile and carries on, leaving finished sections alone, plus an optional Etherwarp Reposition that warps you to each puzzle's standing spots. Auto Water Board clicks each timed lever on the tick the Water Board Solver's countdown reaches it: as soon as you stand where the next lever is in reach your body is already turned onto it, and the tick a lever is clicked your body turns to whatever comes next (the next lever, or the etherwarp toward it), so the warp goes the very next tick; when two clicks come too close together for a warp in between, it stands where both are in reach. Every other auto that warps or clicks works the same way (since 2026-10-15, no fixed waits): an etherwarp's use goes the tick after its sneak goes down (or in the same tick when the sneak is already held, as between the bow puzzles' warps), and the tick after a landing comes the next shot, click or warp - no 2-tick sneak wait, no 2-tick wait after the landing and no 6-tick pause after the teleport; a click on a block (a reward chest, Higher/Lower's secret chest, a Tic Tac Toe square after a warp) has the body already turned onto it in the landing tick, so it needs no turn tick of its own, and an auto's own free camera no longer makes its click wait for "somebody else" turning you. Auto Tic Tac Toe clicks a new move the tick after it reads it (only a retry of the same square still waits 500 ms) and starts the Grab Secret warp in the tick of its first click, and the warp back the tick the chest opens. Once Water Board, Ice Path or Ice Fill is solved, the auto opens the puzzle's reward chest itself (Secret Aura skips Ice Path and Ice Fill and holds off in Water Board), warping into reach of it first if it has to. A chest counts as opened the moment the server sends its window, even when Auto Close Chest hides it, so each chest is clicked once and the step ends that tick; a click with no answer is tried again as soon as the answer is overdue (your measured ping plus any server lag), and once a chest has been dealt with the auto never waits for another to appear. Autopilot, Auto Secret and Auto Routes carry on the tick that last step is over (Boulder's walk out, Higher/Lower's last kill and secret, Creeper Beams' fourth pair, the Three Weirdos chest, Quiz's final answer, the end of a Tic Tac Toe game, a Teleport Maze walk out, a reward chest), and also the tick Hypixel's "PUZZLE SOLVED!" line for you arrives rather than waiting for the map or tab list to show it, and while they wait on an auto puzzle each logs so once per room. From an auto's first aim or warp to its last (Blaze, Creeper Beams, Ice Path, Ice Fill, Water Board, Teleport Maze), your camera is in AP3's free camera: your body turns to every shot and warp exactly as the server sees it, your screen keeps your view (Higher/Lower starts it facing the room's center) and your mouse moves only the view; when the auto finishes or you leave the room, your body turns back to where you are looking and the camera is handed back with nothing moving. The shooting autos find their own shot: Auto Ice Path warps straight onto the silverfish's cell when it can see it, otherwise to a board cell that can, otherwise walks there with the Interactive Map; Auto Blaze judges spots from your real eye height, searches the room's ledges when none of the usual spots has a line, never sends a second volley before the first one's result is in, and moves on the moment it is (the target's kill reported, or none of the volley's arrows able to reach it any more - hit, stuck, or flying away; since 2026-10-15 there is no fixed margin on top), dropping a blaze whose kill is already reported from the order at once, and gives a spot up after three shots that kill nothing (a volley has no spread, so another from the same block flies the same way); with a Terminator it also takes a shot whose side arrow (5 degrees either side) is the one that reaches the blaze, when the center arrow has no clean line, picks the standing spot that can shoot the most blazes in a row, and when the next blaze has no shot from where you stand it warps to its spot while the volley at the current one is still in the air; the Shoot cooldown is counted in whole ticks (500 ms is exactly 10), and the room is done the tick the last blaze dies to its volley rather than 30 ticks later; with its **Auto Secret** option on it sets off for the room's secret the tick after the volley at the last blaze leaves the bow, without waiting for the kill, walks to the block within aura reach of the chest that is nearest the room's door rather than onto the chest, and takes it once that volley has landed (it waits at a chest that only appears when the room is done); if that last blaze survives, it still takes the secret first and then goes back and shoots until the room is done; Auto Creeper Beams aims at each lantern's center from where you actually stand, and with no clear line past the creeper it shoots anyway and logs so (on Hypixel that arrow can hit the creeper). Its **Creeper Beams Mode** setting (Shoot by default) can be set to Melee: instead of the bow it etherwarps to a spot within reach of each lantern (4.5 blocks to the lantern's box, with a clear look at it) and left-clicks it. A lantern no such spot reaches (one hung high up) is reached with a plain Aspect of the Void teleport that lands him beside it, and hit in the air the tick after; one neither reaches is shot with the shortbow, from where he stands or from a spot with a clear line that misses the creeper. It starts the cheapest pair it can finish (spots first, then teleports, shots last); a hit with no answer from the room (the hurt sound or the lantern changing) is tried again another way, and it never stops while a lit pair can still be finished. **Auto Puzzle Pathing** is one switch over every piece of walking the auto puzzles do - Water Board's start-area warp, Boulder's trip above the chest, Tic Tac Toe's room spot and chest trip, Higher/Lower's walk to the secret, and the walk out of a finished room. On by default, and with it off the solvers and their clicking still work but nothing moves you. Auto Tic Tac Toe also gained **Walk Out When Done**, off by default: when the game is over (a line of three or a full board, or the puzzle solved or failed on the tab list - not merely while the computer is thinking about its reply) it asks the live map for the nearest door to the room you are in and paths there, which works in any room rather than needing a hand-measured exit per room, and stands two blocks back from a locked door. It is off by default because it is the one auto-puzzle walk whose destination nobody has stood on and checked. Auto Quiz presses one of the correct pillar's **buttons** rather than the pillar itself, which is a plain smooth-stone block a right-click does nothing to. Every auto puzzle's wait for the server (an etherwarp reposition landing, a pad's teleport in the Teleport Maze, a Boulder button or box, Ice Fill's regenerating section, the Blaze chest) is extended by any server lag, so a lag spike does not end a solve; with no lag the timing is unchanged.
- Auto Dialogue / Breaker Aura — **cheat build only.** Picks the first option of every NPC dialogue it can (no NPC-name list since 2026-11-09; never purchases or trades; dungeons only unless "Outside Dungeons" is on), and breaks blocks you have PICKED with the Dungeonbreaker - **in a boss fight only** (the F7/M7 boss phase or any floor's boss room); during clear it breaks nothing, and the only automatic breaking is an Auto Routes breaker node. **Pick Block Key** (`;` by default, rebindable in the Breaker Aura tab): look at a block and press it to mark it; press it again on a marked block to unmark it. Marked blocks are drawn in orange, brighter once they are in reach, and they survive a restart. **Display** chooses how: Highlight (the default) draws them like any block outline, hidden behind walls; Waypoint draws every picked block through walls whenever it is within your render distance, however far out of reach. **Box Style** makes them Outline (the default), Fill, or Filled Outline, in either display. Broken picks are not drawn. Nothing else is ever broken: there is no automatic path mode any more (the old **Only Picked Blocks** switch and its **Side Reach** slider were removed 2026-10-05). A picked block breaks as soon as it is within your **Reach** - ahead of you, to your side, or the block you are standing on. The block you are standing on always disappears on your screen the moment its break is sent, Zero Ping or not, exactly as a block you break instantly by hand does, so you start falling on the same tick (with it kept until the server answered, GrimAC flagged the gap as standing on air). Picking works with the aura switched off, so you can mark a wall first and turn it on after, and it is disabled in Auto Routes edit mode so placing nodes cannot mark or break anything. It breaks one block a tick, the blocks in front of you first, and only a block your look (as the server last heard it) passes through: to reach the next pick it tilts your look up or down (the camera stays where you had it), and while you walk it turns your body sideways only in 45-degree steps with your movement keys turned to match, so you go exactly where your own keys take you (never on a sprint jump's take-off tick or while AP3 is aiming you); a pick no such look reaches waits until one does. After the switch come three rows: Zero Ping, Legit Multi Break and Burst Multi Break (flags Grim); the two Multi Breaks are one or the other, turning one on turns the other off. Legit Multi Break (cheat build, on by default) breaks every picked block in reach back to back, one each tick with no cooldown between them, and Auto Routes' breaker nodes do the same; it flags no GrimAC check, default or experimental. With both off, Breaker Aura waits its Cooldown after every block and a breaker node in legit mode its Interact Delay. Until 2026-11-02 Multi Break sent every block on one tick, which GrimAC's MultiBreak check flags: it allows one block per server tick, and a client sends one tick a tick, so one a tick is the fastest pace that does not flag. Burst Multi Break (flags Grim), off by default, is that old burst exactly: every picked block in reach goes in the same tick on whatever look you have (no turn, no waiting for an aim), charges the only limit, then the Cooldown; breaker nodes burst too. GrimAC's experimental checks flag it (MultiBreak, and RotationBreak on a block your look misses); Hypixel is not known to check them. A saved Multi Break carries over as Legit Multi Break, and a saved Burst as Burst. At one a tick a sprint with Speed into a long wall can still reach blocks still being removed. A picked block counts as air to the AP3 route planner, so a route may be planned straight through a wall you intend to break
- Custom Party Finder — our own party finder, shared by every mod user through the mod's relay (/cpf, or Open Party Finder in its card). It opens on a start screen where you pick Dungeons, Diana, Fishing or Misc (any party that fits none of the others, with a typed activity); inside one, only its listings show, with a Back button to the start screen. Each listing shows the leader, the activity, the party size, its requirements and a note. A search box matches the leader's name, the activity and the note; filters show one floor only, only parties whose requirements you meet, and hide full parties; the sort is newest or oldest first, lowest or highest Cata Level requirement, or easiest or hardest S+ PB requirement. The sort and filters are remembered (and ride in profiles). Create lists your party: the activity, party size, requirements (Cata Level, Secrets and an S+ PB for the floor, as m:ss, for dungeons) and note start from what you used last there. Join on someone's listing asks the leader through the relay; with Check Requirements on, your own stats are checked first, and a party whose requirements you are under - or whose requirements cannot be checked yet because your stats are still loading - is not asked. The relay refuses the same joins itself, so they never reach the leader. The leader sees each request with the joiner's stats against his requirements: one under any of them (or whose stats could not be read) shows as rejected with no Accept and the joiner is told; one that meets them has Accept and Decline, and with Auto Accept Requests on (the default) is invited straight away. Accept sends them a Hypixel party invite; with Auto Accept Invite on, the joiner's client accepts that leader's invite for them, once, within a minute. With Dual-List Hypixel Queues on, queuing a dungeon in Hypixel's Party Finder lists the same party here too. A listing goes when the party fills, when the party is left or disbanded, when the run starts, when you leave the server, or with Unlist (or /cpf unlist). Party commands are limited hard: invites at most one every 3 seconds and six a minute (the same name once per 30 seconds), and an accept only for a leader who accepted your own request.
- Dungeon Queue — Auto Requeue, PV Link on Join and Party Finder Overlay are three features with a switch each, and the menu card is ON while any is on and OFF only when all are (clicking it when all are off turns on Auto Requeue only). PV Link on Join (default OFF): when Hypixel says "Party Finder > Name joined the dungeon group! (Class Level N)", or "Name joined the party." while you are in a dungeon or have a Party Finder queue, a local line ">> [PV] Name - click to open their profile" appears under it in the menu theme's accent color; clicking anywhere on the line runs /pv Name. Only the real Hypixel lines count, a player typing the same words adds nothing, and your own join adds nothing. Auto Requeue (sends /instancerequeue at the end of a run after a delay, skipped if a party member leaves or is kicked) and Party Finder Overlay (green/red joinable highlight, member count, and tooltip stats: Catacombs level, secrets, PB for the floor, missing classes), with a live style preview, always shown at the bottom of its settings, that goes through the same code as the real tooltip; "Compact" is now called Party Finder style. Built-in styles: None, Style 1, Style 2, Compact, Minimal, Detailed, Stat Row and Class Tag. Custom is a style you write from placeholders ($Name, $NameColor, $Role, $RoleShort, $RoleSingle, $RoleColor, $RoleLevel, $Cata, $Secrets, $SecretsShort, $SecretAvg, $SecretShortAvg, $PB; & is a color code): placeholder buttons add themselves to the text, Load Built-in copies any built-in style in to start from, a line names any $Word that is not a placeholder, and Save Style / Saved Style / Delete keep any number of named custom styles (up to 32) that you step through with the Saved Style button The stats come first from killer560's relay, which keeps one cache shared by every user of the mod: the whole page goes in one request, anyone looked at recently shows at once, and the rest fill in as the relay fetches them (it polls every second or two, and a player older than 15 minutes in the cache shows straight away and updates a few seconds later if their PB changed). At the same time the mod fetches players itself from the Profile Viewer's source (the keyless SkyBlockPV backend, or your own Hypixel key if you set one there), three at a time, starting from the players the relay will reach last so the two meet in the middle instead of fetching the same people; it waits out the backend's rate limit (a 429 and its Retry-After) and retries a failed fetch after 15 s, 30 s, 1 and 2 minutes rather than showing ? for three minutes. The party you hover goes to the front of both (so does Auto Kick's joiner), then the rest of the open page, then anyone from earlier pages. Lookups keep running after you close the menu, so reopening it shows what arrived meanwhile; they stop when you change world (a dungeon starting, a lobby swap) or after 10 minutes. Answers are saved to disk for 3 days, so players you have seen before show at once and refresh in the background. Either way: Catacombs level from the selected profile (or, when none is selected or it has no dungeon data, the profile with the most Catacombs XP), secrets and average secrets over all profiles, and that profile's PB for the party's floor (Master Mode for a Master Mode party; Entrance too) - S+, S, or in Both mode S+ then S then the fastest clear at any score, shown gray and labeled "Any". Member lines are always in the chosen style, with ? for any stat not yet loaded (and a line under them saying it is still loading, or which source failed and why). Warns once if Devonian's own Party Finder Overview is on, since it replaces ours
- Creation Cooldown — SkyHanni's Dungeon Creation Cooldown (to-do Doc, 2026-11-01). Hypixel will not create another dungeon for 30 s after you enter one; the timer starts on your own "<you> entered The Catacombs, Floor N!" line (another player's line, or anyone's chat, never starts it). Creation Cooldown (the HUD, off by default) shows "Creation Cooldown: 12s" while it runs, movable in the HUD editor: in a dungeon, and with Only In Green Room (on by default) only while you stand in the green Entrance room (or before the map knows any room); with Show On All Islands (off) also anywhere else on Skyblock. Chat When Over (off) says so in chat when it ends. Block Instance Creation (its own switch, off by default) holds back /joininstance and /joindungeon while it runs, with a chat line saying how long is left: typed ones, the /f7-style shortcuts, Party Commands' !f7 and Insta Clear Warning's requeue alike. The card is ON while either switch is on. In Dungeons
- Blood Rush Helper — (killer560, 2026-11-03; off by default; Dungeon category, its own card) shows where to go to kill the blood rush mobs, the reference mod adm's BR Roles done our own way (our code, our data). On the rush a room's Wither Key drops only once its starred mobs are dead, so it boxes them, shares them between the killers, numbers yours in kill order, fills in the next one and drops a box once its mobs die. From the start of the run until "The BLOOD DOOR has been opened!", when every box goes; a room the map shows cleared shows none. **Which rooms:** the one you are in and, once a wither door into it opens, the next room on the rush, when they are mob rooms on the rush path - the rooms on the way from the Entrance to each wither or blood door the dungeon map in your hotbar shows (**All Rooms** shows every mob room). **Boxes:** the room's known spawn spots grouped within 3.5 blocks - the ones you learned plus the table the mod ships with (built from spots learned on real Hypixel runs; since 2026-11-04 it covers only the rooms learned so far, and grows as the table is rebuilt), so a room it knows shows its boxes before its mobs are in view; a starred mob is claimed by the box its body overlapped most where it was first seen, else the nearest within 5 blocks, and mobs no box claims make boxes of their own. A box shows until every mob it claimed is dead: Hypixel sends no death, so a mob removed within 32 blocks of you, or within 48 on the same tick as its name tag, is dead, and further off it is only out of view. **The split** keys on the door you came in by (the doorway you last walked through, else the room's door on the Entrance side of the path): one area of the room a killer, as even in boxes as the room allows on normal floors; role 1 is the area farthest from that door, role 2 the next (in master mode the area with the most boxes is role 1); inside your area the order goes from the door, nearest first, then each next the nearest to the last. A room with one box is everyone's. Once your boxes are done, the others' left show in gold as help. adm keeps hand-made plans per room, door and team size; ours is a rule fitted to them and agrees on about 64% of boxes with two killers (53% with three, 56% with four; 55% and 50% in M7), so it is close, not identical. **Roles,** numbered as adm numbers them: **Killers** (Just Me, 2, 3, 4; the door runner not counted) and **My Role** (1 to Killers, or Door, who kills nothing). In master mode your class picks your role - Archer 1, Mage 2, Berserk 3, Tank 4, the Healer on the door. adm's party chat lines set them for a run and override both: `!3br 2` (three killing, I am role 2), `!br 2` (role 2 at the count already set), `!br d` (I am on the door); a different count starts over, and claims last until the dungeon is over. Only Hypixel's own `Party > ` lines count, never any other chat, and the mod never sends one. While anyone has claimed a role that way, a movable HUD line (Blood Rush Roles) says "ADM players in party: our split is close to ADM's, not identical - call your rooms" with who claimed what, and the others' boxes carry the claimer's name. **Door Kills** (Never, Always, After Fairy) has the door runner kill too, as one more role, the last; with After Fairy only in rooms the rush reaches after fairy, and **Drop Bottom After Fairy** has the highest numbered killer leave the rush there to clear (a chat line only you see says so). **Show Others' Spots** (on) draws the other roles' boxes in gray marked R2, R3... **Learn Spawn Spots** (on) remembers where each room's starred mobs spawn - the block one stood on when first seen with no player within 10 blocks of it, once it has stood there half a second - in the room's own coordinates, so a spot comes back at any rotation in later runs; it learns all run long until the boss (since 2026-11-04; before, only on the rush), so the clear's rooms are learned too; it also learns while the helper itself is OFF (its row stays in the tab and is one of the card's master toggles, so the card reads ON while it learns), so ordinary runs fill the table; only on Hypixel, never in the Dungeon Sim. **Show Learned Spots** (on) draws the known spots too, in the Learned Spot Color until a mob is on them. **Hide Secret Waypoints** (off) hides Secret Waypoints while you stand in a room on the rush path, until the blood door opens; other rooms show as normal. **Forget Learned Spots** deletes the ones you learned (`killer560smod-bloodrush-spots.json`, kept out of profiles); the shipped ones stay. Colors for your boxes, the others' and learned ones, **Filled Boxes** (on; off draws outlines only), **Fill Opacity** (25% by default; the next box is filled more) and **Outline Width** (1-10, 4 by default). Legit, the boxes and numbers are depth-tested and the rush is read off the hotbar map. Cheat build only: **Through Walls**, and **Whole Rush Path** - every room on the rush path from the scanned dungeon at once, including rooms the hotbar map does not show yet. It shows only: it never moves, aims, clicks or chats. Works in the Dungeon Sim on a floor with Spawn Starred Mobs on.
- Insta Clear Warning — (to-do Doc, 2026-11-01; off by default) warns when a room on the way to blood is insta cleared: the room in front of a shut wither door (the fairy room's door is one) or the blood door, which the map shows cleared while nobody picked up its key and no key lies on the ground - Hypixel drops the key at the room's last starred death, so a room that clears without one has lost it. After a room flips to cleared the mod waits 3 s for its key (a key of that kind seen on the ground, the team's key count going up on the sidebar or in the "has obtained" lines, or the door opening all end it quietly), and warns only while you are within 48 blocks of the room: farther away the client may not see the key, so it waits for you to come close (up to a minute) rather than guess. Once per room. HUD Popup (on) shows "Blood rush room was insta cleared!" and the room for 6 s with a sound, movable in the HUD editor. Party Message (on) sends your line to party chat, by default "Blood rush room was instacleared we must restart" (empty puts the default back; section signs and control characters are left out). Requeue Same Floor (off) sends /joininstance for the floor you are on once the 30 s creation cooldown (Creation Cooldown) and Auto Requeue's Requeue Delay have passed, once a run. In Dungeons
- Armor HUD — a movable, scalable HUD panel (off by default) showing the four armor pieces you are wearing (helmet, chestplate, leggings, boots) as item icons, read from your real armor slots. Show Equipment (on by default) adds Skyblock's four equipment pieces (necklace, cloak, belt, gloves or bracelet): they are not in your inventory, so the mod reads them whenever Hypixel's equipment menu ("Your Equipment and Stats", from /stats or the SkyBlock Menu) is open, keeps them in a cache file across restarts (not carried in profiles), and shows the last ones it saw; a slot it has never seen stays blank, and an empty slot in the menu clears it. Layout steps between Vertical (armor in one column, equipment in a second beside it) and Horizontal (two rows). Durability Bars draws each item's durability bar, and Background turns the dark panel and its outline on or off. Skyblock Only, like every HUD. Every setting is kept across restarts and in profiles.
- Inventory HUD — your main inventory drawn as a movable HUD panel (mini/normal, horizontal/vertical, background style, show always / hold key / toggle key). Until you move it in the HUD editor it sits centered above your hotbar, a little way up. The Opacity slider fades the panel background, outline and slots (0 hides the panel); the items are never dimmed - item textures cannot be tinted on 26.1.2, and the black overlay that used to fake it left a black box at any setting below 100, so it was removed on 2026-10-18.
- Inventory Walk — **cheat build only, off by default.** Your movement keys keep working while a chest, your inventory or any other container (or, with Screens: All Screens, any screen) is open - never while a text box has focus, and never sneak (shift stays the screen's shift-click). Jump and Sprint can be switched off. Stop To Click (on): a slot click or closing the menu while you move waits one tick: that tick you stand still (whatever is driving you, an Auto Route too), the server hears it, then the click or close goes and you walk on - a click while the server has you moving or sprinting is GrimAC's MultiActionsC (which also throws the click away, so the item never moves), a close is MultiActionsD. A click the very moment a menu opens waits the same way, and during a Blink hold the click joins the hold behind your still tick. Flying (a dungeon ghost) counts: hovering with no keys is standing still. Hypixel closes a Floor 7 terminal (and other server menus such as the SkyBlock Menu and Storage) by itself as soon as it hears a movement key from you. Your keys stay honest in server menus too (the Stand Still In Server Menus option was removed): Hypixel may close such a menu when it hears them, and every close by the server writes one "[InvWalk] server closed" line to the log (ticks open, when your keys went down, your last click, how far you moved), so the next log shows exactly what it closes on. An anvil opens with its rename box focused, so you do not walk there. In your own inventory you keep walking with an item on the cursor: the pick-up waits, unsent, until the click that puts it down, and then you stand still for one tick and the clicks go with a close of the inventory, as a vanilla client sends them (a click there followed by walking with no close is what Hypixel limbos as "Bad client inventory move"). Pause After Click lets the keys go for a few more ticks after each click. Look Key (Not Set by default): hold it with a menu open and moving the mouse turns you at your normal sensitivity while the cursor, the hovered slot and its tooltip stay exactly where they were, so nothing in the menu is hovered or clicked; let go and the mouse moves the cursor again from that same spot (never while a text box has focus). Look Key Mode (Hold by default): Toggle makes one press start looking and the next press stop it; closing the menu stops it too. Tested against GrimAC only. Carry In Chests (off by default, needs Stop To Click): in a chest, large chest, ender chest, barrel or a Hypixel Ender Chest page or backpack (not its top row of buttons), an item you pick up stays on your cursor without being sent while you keep walking; when you put it down (or click anything else) you stop for a moment and the clicks go together, so the server never has an item on its cursor while you move. Every other server menu is buttons and keeps the plain stop. If the server resets your cursor before the put-down (a resync or a new menu), the unsent clicks are dropped and the menu shows what the server has.
- Skyblock Only — one toggle on the Home tab that pauses every feature outside Hypixel Skyblock and p3sim.net without changing any of your settings; rejoin Skyblock and everything is back on
- Sweat Mode — Enters sweat mode.
- Correction Alarm — **cheat build only.** A mod-wide rule since 2026-10-06: a server position correction never stops, pauses or switches off any automation (AP3, Auto Routes, the Interactive Map's runner, Auto Boulder, Auto Walk and the rest). Each one posts a chat line saying which feature, what it was doing and how far you were moved (at most one line every two seconds per feature, folding the rest into a count), plays an original siren-style alarm (at most once every three seconds), and the feature carries on from where the server put you, re-planning if it plans paths. Nothing ever moves you back. Only a move the server made on its own counts: the landing of a warp another of your features just sent (Auto Ice Fill's hops, a puzzle's reward chest warp) is that feature's own, so an Interactive Map trip that has already landed and is settling when one of them hops simply ends as arrived - no line, no alarm. Correction Alarm (on/off) and Alarm Volume (0-100%, default 100%) are on the Home tab.
- Freelook — the camera trick other mods call Freelook (Freelook OG, Free Look UTI, Decent Freelook; Shoulder Check and SimpleLook are the same idea). With it on, F5 (whatever key is bound to Toggle Perspective) goes straight to the backward view, the camera in front of you looking back, and back to the view you had, instead of cycling all three. Mode Hold keeps the backward view while F5 is held; Toggle switches on each press. Hold the Pan Key (a key or mouse button) to swing the camera around you with the mouse - from first person it shows the normal third-person view while held - and let go to have it return behind you. Camera only: your facing, movement and everything sent to the server stay exactly as they were. Off and the Pan Key unbound until you set them in Misc > Freelook
- Dark Mode — darkens literally everything with ONE black fill at Opacity % (1-80, default 25), drawn once at the very end of the GUI frame: the world, HUD, chat, inventory, every container and menu, the title screen and pause menu, tooltips, this mod's menu and screens, all at the same level, so opening a screen never changes how dark it is. (Until 2026-11-10 the chat and the mod's own screens were tinted a second time and vanilla screens not at all; the Tint HUD switch is gone and an old saved key is ignored.) Idea from NoammAddons (CC0).
- Motion Blur — smooth frame-blending blur for high-FPS recordings (strength slider, optional GUI blur); turns itself off with a chat notice if the shader can't load
- Window Layout — pick how many game windows share a monitor, then click a cell in the picker to snap this window there at the right size (per-instance memory, optional restore on launch, taskbar-aware). Drawn in the mod menu's theme; its own Theme setting starts on Theme Default (follows the menu) and can be set to Amber, Dark, Light, Ocean, Viridian or your Custom colors for this screen alone
- Real Time — a HUD clock from your computer's time (12h/24h, seconds, zone abbreviation) or any custom time zone
- Custom Scoreboard — SkyHanni-style replacement sidebar (ported from SkyHanni and SkyBlock Custom Scoreboard). It looks like a tidier vanilla sidebar: compact lines on a plain translucent box, with the title row a little darker, a thin orange border (Border Style Custom, on by default) and square corners. It sits where the vanilla scoreboard does - against the right edge of the screen, vertically centered - at every GUI scale and window size, and grows to the left when a line gets longer: its right wall, and the right end of its widest line, stay on exactly the same pixel whatever the lines, Text Align, Min Width, HUD scale or GUI scale (on the left edge, the same holds for its left wall and the left end of its text). Drag it anywhere in Edit HUD: dropped on the left half it anchors to the left edge instead, and it can never be drawn or dragged past any edge of the screen (a position saved by an older version that hung off screen is pulled back on). Reset Position (in the editor's Settings) puts it back on the right. Unknown lines still show (each is written to the log once; Unknown Line Warning, off by default, also notes it in chat once per kind of line); the dungeon board's "Solo" and "Keys:" lines are recognized however Hypixel colors them. Everything is edited in **Open Visual Editor** (the tab itself is just the on/off switch and that button): the board is drawn there filled with sample lines, and you drag the lines on the board itself to reorder them (an orange line shows where one will land). Drag a line onto the right-hand panel to take it off; the panel's **Not Active** list holds everything that is not on the board - click one to add it at the bottom, or drag it onto the board where it should go. Click a line on the board for its own settings (Mayor's perks and minister, Maxwell Tuning's amount, Party's leader and size, the Title and Footer text and alignment, and so on; Events and Chunked Stats show their own drag-to-order lists). The **Settings** page has what most people change, in this order: Theme, Text Align, Reset Position, Hide With Tab List, Hide With Chat Open, Hide In Menus, Numbers and Number Style, Line Spacing, Text Shadow, Image Background, Image Opacity, Reload Image, Open Image Folder, 24h SkyBlock Time, Date Format, Outside Skyblock and Clickable Lines - and one **Advanced** section, closed until you open it, with the rest: Hide Vanilla Scoreboard, Custom Lines, the empty, irrelevant and separator line rules, Cache On Island Switch, Show Number Changes, Unknown Line Warning, and the Box (Background, its color and opacity, padding, rounded corners, border with gradient or a Chroma Border whose rainbow flows diagonally round it, border softness, background blur, min width and height). Settings and boards saved before these changes load unchanged; a saved board still on the old orange look moves to the new one, keeping any look setting you had changed.
- Profile Viewer — `/pv [name]`: NEU-style Skyblock profile viewer (skills, slayers, dungeons with floor times, inventories/ender chest/backpacks/wardrobe/accessories, pets) with a 3D skin preview; a Weight page with Senither and Lily weight (skill/slayer/dungeon breakdown with overflow); source defaults to Auto, a recent-views sidebar (your own head pinned first, click a head or type a name to switch), Ender Chest and Backpacks tiled several pages at once, click-to-select Bestiary categories, and overflow levels on maxed skills - the number in brackets is the level your XP would reach with no cap at all, on the standard 60-level curve and past 60 the way SkyHanni counts it (level 61 costs 7.6M XP, each next level 600K more, that increase doubling every ten levels); Runecrafting and Social stop at their own tables. Every page tab fits on two rows. The name box under Switch is wide enough for a 16-character name and tab-completes: start typing and it lists matching names from the tab list, your recent views and anyone looked up this session; Tab cycles them (Shift+Tab back), a click opens one. Ender Chest pages and Backpacks are drawn as Storage Overlay panels (its theme, no backpack icon) and are clipped at the bottom of the page instead of running off it. Items show the Item Rarity background when that feature is on. Basic Info has a Networth line under Bank (the same estimate as the Networth page; click it to open that page), priced with the Price Tooltip's Est. Item Value (Networth mode: enchants, Recombobulators, potato books, stars, gemstones, scrolls, drill and rod parts, dye, skin and rune all count, times the stack size; an item with no known price falls back to its Bazaar or lowest BIN price) with no API key needed. Non-Cosmetic Networth (Profile Viewer tab, off by default, kept across restarts; the Networth page also has a button for it) makes the Networth line and page show the total without cosmetic value: pet skins, helmet and armor skins, applied dyes and runes, and items that are only cosmetic (dyes, runes, skins); the Networth page lists both totals either way. The data's age is on the Refresh button's hover. Drawn in the mod menu's theme; its own Theme setting starts on Theme Default (follows the menu) and can be set to Amber, Dark, Light, Ocean, Viridian or your Custom colors for this screen alone (the Storage Overlay panels keep the Storage Overlay's own theme). Style (Profile Viewer tab, and the Style button in the /pv header: left click the next, right click the one before; saved at once, kept across restarts and in profiles) picks how /pv is laid out and drawn: Default (the look above), NEU Classic (exactly NotEnoughUpdates' old 1.8.9 profile viewer: its own 431 x 202 panel texture over a blurred world, centered, its 28-pixel tabs from the panel's left corner with the unpressed ones behind the panel and the pressed one in front, the page name on hover, and the Profile, Style and Refresh buttons as its 100 x 20 dropdowns under the panel), SkyBlockPV Light, SkyBlockPV Dark and SkyBlockPV Transparent (exactly the Default, Dark and NEU themes of the SkyBlockPV mod: its own panel, box and tab sprites, a panel 65% of the screen high at 16:9, 20-pixel tabs from 20 pixels in with the selected one green, the profile button under the panel's left corner, Style and Refresh at the screen's top left, its gray text, and the page name under the panel when there is room; only Transparent blurs the world). The styles draw the original mods' own sprites (bundled, about 14 KB, loaded once with the GUI atlas); any style but Default brings its own colors in place of the Theme setting. Based on: NEU's GuiProfileViewer and its pv_bg/pv_elements/pv_dung/pv_dropdown sprites (LGPL-3.0), SkyBlockPV's BaseWindowedPvScreen and theme sprites (meowdding/skyblock-pv, code MIT with attribution, assets CC BY 4.0) and Olympus' dark button (MIT); credited in THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md. The SkyBlockPV styles draw their text without a shadow, as SkyBlockPV does, and every style uses that mod’s own item slot. In NEU Classic, Basic Info and Dungeons lie on NEU’s own page textures (pv_basic, pv_dung) with the skin in its model frame, the stats in its middle box and skills and slayers in its right box (Basic), and the Catacombs level, run stats, selected class, floor table and classes in its five boxes (Dungeons); Inventories lies on pv_invs (NEU’s selector grid of seven, armor and equipment in its two columns, the inventory in a chest panel), Pets on pv_pets (its 5 x 4 grid of 20 a page with the active or pinned pet tinted orange, the pet drawn large in the white frame and its stats on the right) and Collections on pv_cols (the categories down the left, a 5 x 4 grid of collections each filled orange by its progress with the tier above and the amount below, and the minions half left empty, as our data has no minion tiers). SkyBlockPV’s Basic Info page is its main page: an Info label on the left (Purse, Bank, SkyBlock Level, First Join, Skill Avg, Fairy Souls, Networth), the player in its frame in the middle fifth with its status button left empty, and Skills and Slayer as rows of level chips on the right (its Essence section is left out: our data has none). Its Inventory page is its own: the categories as tabs on the panel’s right edge, and armor and equipment in a 2 x 4 block beside the 9 x 4 inventory (hotbar last), centered, with no boxes; its Pets page has its 22 px slots over the left 65 % (the selected pet tinted green, each level in its rarity color) and the Info label on the right; its Collections page has the categories as the same right-edge tabs and each category's collections two to a row, each with its 91 px progress bar and "N% to tier" or Maxed (the Minion tab is left out: our data has no minion tiers). Kept in our own layout, because our pages hold different data: NEU Classic’s other pages (Mining, Bestiary, Crimson Isle, Museum, Rift, Farming, Networth, Weight) and the other SkyBlockPV pages’ contents
- Themed Main Menu — the title screen redrawn in the mod's themes: an animated background, a framed panel holding the button column (Singleplayer, Multiplayer, Dungeon Sim, Mod Settings, Mods, Swap Accounts, then Options and Quit under a thin rule; no Realms, language, accessibility, Friends or Mod Menu buttons: Mods opens the mod's own Mods Screen instead), buttons with an accent ring, glow and side bar on hover, a "Playing as" chip with a green dot under the panel, and a thin accent line along the very bottom edge; the version line, the mod's name and version, and the copyright line are not shown. "Playing as" always shows the account's real name: Name Changer never recolors or renames anything on the main menu or the Swap Accounts screens. Every theme has two backgrounds, picked by Main Menu Scene (below): Fancy, its painted scene, and Simple, just floating particles in the theme's own colors and shading. The Simple scenes are one family: a dark backdrop in the theme's colors, a low glow along the bottom that breathes slowly, a fainter pool of light drifting beside it, and particles at three depths that thin out behind the buttons and stay clear of the title - Amber embers rising and flickering (the far ones tiny and dim, the near ones with a hot center and a soft halo) over a warm floor of light; Ocean bubbles rising and wobbling (each a faint body, a bright rim and a small highlight) with tiny motes drifting in the water, light from the surface above and a deep teal glow below; Dark a fine field of stars twinkling and drifting, dust rising slowly, a few soft near motes, two faint wandering nebula glows and the odd shooting star, nearly black and white; Viridian fireflies wandering and blinking in yellow-green with wide soft glows, and spores drifting down, over a mossy glow under a darker canopy. They are drawn in HD by the mod's own shaders at the screen's full resolution: every particle exactly antialiased and gliding between pixels, every glow and gradient dithered so nothing bands, and all of it moving smoothly at 120 FPS. The Fancy scenes: Amber's Aurora, killer560's own picture of an orange aurora over a blocky valley at sunset, with the baked-in title and buttons removed and every aurora band followed across the gaps they left, animated through a shader so all of it is smooth at full resolution - the curtains ripple gently, each ray flickers softly, light travels slowly along the bands, the stars twinkle, the low sun's glow breathes, glints drift on the river, the lava on the right mountain pulses and a few embers drift up at the sides; the view itself never moves; the older Amber scenes - the drifting embers, NEW, a dungeon colonnade at dusk, and the first calm embers - stay in the code behind `MainMenuScenes.AMBER_SCENE`), Viridian the theme designer's own picture of the slime boss, animated (drawn from a 2560x1440 copy of it, smoothly filtered, with its glows and drifting fog smooth and dithered, and the waterfall streaks and lake shimmer gliding between pixels), or a slime boss (the acid cave below, with a huge glowing Minecraft slime crowned with light rising out of the lake under the waterfall and a big close-up silhouette of the theme designer's character facing it, sword in hand; a still for now) or an acid cave (blocky cave walls and stalactites, some dripping, framing a glowing acid lake with drifting ripples, bubbles that rise and pop and rings on its surface, an acid waterfall pouring from the ceiling with a foaming splash and rising mist, green fog drifting over it all, and on a ledge bottom left the silhouette of the theme designer's cat-eared character swinging a sword at a big Minecraft slime that hops, squishes and flashes when hit; the earlier forest at dusk and the simpler fireflies scene are kept in the code), Ocean Cavern Falls (killer560's own picture of a dark teal Minecraft cavern, animated through a shader - the whole curtain of water pouring down (every streak of the picture lit by dashes rushing down its own column, turbulent sheets and surges rolling down the falls, fine fast threads in front, spray churning up where it lands), the lake itself moving (waves rolling toward the viewer and rings spreading from where the falls land bend its reflections, the falls' reflection shimmers and stretches, current lines drift across, glints ride the crests) and the mist at the foot billowing, drawn from a 2560x1440 texture, bright soft streaks falling in front and fading into the mist, the small falls on the cliffs running with foam at their feet, mist billowing and drifting where it all lands, ripples spreading and glints coming and going on the dark lake, the glowing specks on the cliffs twinkling and motes of spray drifting up, the feet of the falls glowing in a slow pulse, the view itself still; the older drawn waterfall stays in the code), Dark a Minecraft-styled Milky Way (killer560's own picture graded almost black and white - a blocky galaxy band with dark dust lanes running down to a bright core low on the right, blocky pines and hills, and a small caped character sitting on a stepped grass-block hill at the lower left looking up - animated: the bright stars twinkle as crisp crosses and faint ones flicker, a blocky shooting star crosses every few seconds away from the title, the core's glow breathes, haze drifts along the hills and her cape flutters; the same picture without the character and the older drifting starfield stay in the code), Light soft drifting lights and slanted rays on a light backdrop, its one scene in both Fancy and Simple (drawn by the mod's own shaders at the screen's full resolution: the rays fade with soft sides, the glow and the vignette are smooth, every gradient is dithered with blue noise so no banding shows on any screen, and the drifting lights are perfectly antialiased discs gliding between pixels at any GUI scale), and Custom the Simple embers in the menu's own Custom colors, in both Fancy and Simple (killer560, 2026-10-16: it has no painted scene of its own). Every background moves on time, not frames, and while the themed background is on screen out of a world the game runs menus at your own Max Framerate (120 by default; a lower setting is kept, Unlimited stays unlimited, VSync still applies) instead of Minecraft's 60 FPS menu cap. In Menu Theme's settings, the Main Menu section starts with the Themed Main Menu ON/OFF switch (also in Home's Mod section), then Main Menu Style picks Match Menu Theme (the main menu uses the mod menu's theme and its background, the default), Separate Theme (a theme of its own: Amber, Dark, Light, Ocean, Viridian or the menu's Custom colors) or Custom (a theme of its own plus Background, Particles Off/Low/Normal/High, Animation Speed 25-200% and Button Panel on/off). Right under it, Main Menu Scene picks Fancy (the default) or Simple for whichever theme the style gives (left click the next, right click back); the choice is saved and also sets the background behind Minecraft's other menus. Custom's Background picker lists every scene: Theme Default (the theme's scene in the Main Menu Scene mode), Amber Aurora, Cavern Falls, Milky Way, Aurora, Soft Light, Slime Boss, Plain, and the Simple Embers, Bubbles, Stardust and Fireflies. The yellow splash text Minecraft draws beside the logo is never shown on the themed main menu, in any theme or style; with the main menu theming off the title screen is vanilla's and keeps it. A Mod Settings button in the column opens the mod menu from the main menu (Escape returns to it); every setting works there, and the HUD editors, which need a world, say "Join a world to use Edit HUD" instead of opening. Themed Vanilla Menus (on by default, in the same section) draws every other menu - Singleplayer and Create New World, Multiplayer and its Add Server and Direct Connection pages, Options and every page under it, the pause menu (the sim's Change Room included), loading, disconnected and confirm screens, the mod list and other mods' screens - in the main menu's theme, following Main Menu Style: out of a world the main menu's own background behind a veil (world and server lists sit on an opaque panel so their text stays readable), and its buttons, text fields, sliders, checkboxes, tabs, lists, scrollbars and tooltips everywhere. A theme change shows at once on whatever menu is open. Nothing is moved or removed (the pause menu's icon row has its own switch, Hide Pause Menu Icons). One switch turns it all off - the first row of Menu Theme's Main Menu section, and a row in Home's Mod section (it was in Display Options until 2026-11-04); while it is off the other Main Menu rows are hidden and keep their values; chests and inventories stay normal. Downloads (2026-11-04): the mod carries only small previews of the painted pictures (Amber's Aurora at full size and medium quality, so a fresh install looks right at once; the others at a quarter size), and the full picture and the theme's music loop download once from the mod's GitHub release when the theme is shown or picked, are checked against their sha256 and kept once per PC in `%USERPROFILE%\.killer560smod\shared\theme-assets`, shared by every instance. Until one is ready, and offline, the upscaled preview shows and no music plays; a failed download is tried again two minutes later at the earliest, and an install that had a theme picked before this downloads it at its first start. Downloads are never deleted on their own. Custom Title Screen (Menu Theme's settings, the same switch as Display Options' Themed Main Menu) turns the whole custom title screen off, Minecraft's own instead, and with it off nothing is ever downloaded. Delete Unused Downloaded Themes (under it) asks first, then deletes every downloaded picture and loop except the ones the main menu uses now and says how many and how much; a deleted theme downloads again when it is picked. Title Screen Switcher (only in the Prism instance "Title Screen GUI", which is for looking at the main menus and never joins a server; it has no setting and every other instance never shows it): the Multiplayer button is replaced by Main Menu Looks, which opens a picker listing every main menu look by theme - Vanilla (the plain title screen), Amber Embers / New / Aurora / Simple, Ocean Waterfall / Cavern Falls / Simple, Dark Milky Way / Simple, Light Soft Light, Viridian Slime Boss / Simple, Custom Simple - with the one on screen lit; a click shows that look at once behind the picker, Done goes back to the title screen, and Saved Look goes back to the instance's own settings. A small button in the top right corner of the title screen names the look on screen and steps through the same list: left click the next, right click the previous. A look picked this way changes no setting, so the next launch starts on the saved one. It is turned on by the marker file `killer560smod-title-screen-gui` in the instance's game folder
- Fonts — Menu Theme's Font setting draws text in another font: Minecraft (the default), four bundled fonts (Lato, Space Mono, VT323 and Press Start 2P, all under the SIL Open Font License, listed in assets/killer560smod/fonts/LICENSES.txt), or any .ttf or .otf you import: Open Fonts Folder opens the folder, drop the file in, and it is in the Font list (Reload Fonts reads the folder again; a file that is not a font says so and Minecraft's font stays). Font Applies To picks Mod Menu Only (the mod's own screens) or Everything (every line of text: chat, the HUD, scoreboards, tooltips, Minecraft's menus and name tags). Font Size goes from 70% to 140%. Characters a font lacks (Hypixel's symbols, accents) still come from Minecraft's font. Kept across restarts and in profiles; imported fonts ride in profiles too
- Theme Extras — Menu Theme's Theme Extras section gives each theme (Amber, Dark, Light, Ocean, Green and Custom) things besides its colors; Extras For picks the theme being set up, starting on the menu's own. Theme Music (default ON; each theme plays its own track): while the main menu is open, the theme it shows plays its track on a loop, and leaving the main menu for a world or a server stops it at once (Options, Singleplayer, Multiplayer and the mod menu opened from the title screen keep it going). Each preset theme has its own loop, original music by the mod's owner, downloaded once when it is first wanted (see Themed Main Menu; silent until it is there); a theme's Music row picks Theme Track, Off, or any bundled loop (Custom has no loop of its own). Play Preview, under it, plays that track once at Music Volume so it can be heard right there; a second press, picking another track or closing the menu stops it, and Minecraft's menu music and the main menu's own track wait while it plays. Music Volume runs 0-100% on its own slider, not the game's Music slider; while a theme track plays, Minecraft's own menu music waits. Import Music takes the sound or video whose path is on the clipboard (Shift + right click the file, Copy as path), as Click Sounds does, and makes it that theme's music; an .ogg is kept as it is, anything else is converted by ffmpeg (which must be installed), its first 10 minutes. Imported music is streamed, never held whole, and Open Music Folder opens where it is kept. Theme Font (default Keep Current): a theme can bring any font the Font setting offers (Minecraft's, the bundled ones or an imported one), used while the mod menu is in that theme; Font Applies To and Font Size stay the Font setting's, and Keep Current leaves the Font setting as it is. Theme Background (default Theme Scene): Import Background takes the video or GIF whose path is on the clipboard (Shift + right click the file, Copy as path), as Click Sounds does, and makes it that theme's main menu background, cropped to fill the screen and looping; Open Backgrounds Folder opens where they are kept: one folder for every instance and Minecraft version on this PC (the shared store, backgrounds), not the instance's own; files an instance had in its old folder move in on first use (the same bytes once, different bytes under both names), and profiles do not carry the files. A GIF is used as it is; a video (and a GIF too big to hold in memory) is turned by ffmpeg, which must be installed, into frames at 20 fps, its first 60 seconds at up to 960x540. Decoding runs off the render thread; until the first frame is ready, and if a file is missing or broken, the theme's own scene shows.
- Arrow Align — F7/M7 third device solver: clicks-needed numbers on each frame, Prevent Misclicks (crouch to override); **cheat build:** Trigger Bot with a **CPS range** (1-20, a fresh random gap between the two ends for every click; an old fixed delay becomes its CPS as the low end of a band two wide) and Aura, which clicks every frame in range that still needs it at an **Aura CPS** you set from 1 to 20 (each gap varies a little, the average is what you set; 20 is one click a game tick). It clicks frames already under your crosshair first and only turns your body, never your camera, for the others, so a frame you are looking at goes at the full rate while each one it has to turn to costs an extra tick. An old Min/Max Delay setting becomes the CPS nearest its middle. Before stage S3 starts the Aura takes the lowest, leftmost arrow to one click from done and leaves it there (**Leave Last Arrow**, on by default), so the device completes on a single click once S3 starts. **Aura Clicks Per Tick** (1-7, default 1) sends several clicks on one frame within one tick, so at 7 every frame is finished in a single tick. **Turn And Click Same Tick** (off by default) turns your body to a frame and clicks it in the same tick instead of turning one tick and clicking the next, so each frame costs one tick; with Clicks Per Tick 7 and Aura CPS 20 a whole device takes one tick per frame that needs clicks. It never clicks two different frames in one tick. Both tested clean against GrimAC. The Trigger Bot, the Aura and Leave Last Arrow each have a **Disable While Crouching** switch (on by default) that stops them while you hold crouch.
- Secret Triggerbot — **cheat build only.** (Since 2026-11-09 it shares the Secret Aura card, with its own switch and toggle key.) Clicks a secret (chest, lever, redstone key, wither essence) when you look at it, after a delay; never re-clicks a looted secret; optional slot swap
- Left Click Opens Secrets — **cheat build only**, off by default (red Secrets folder). In a dungeon, your own left click on a secret - a chest, trapped chest, a lever that is not a dummy, Wither Essence or a Redstone Key - is one ordinary right click on it instead of an attack, whatever you are holding (empty hand, a sword, the Dungeon Breaker, anything): nothing is mined, no dig starts, and the chest opens or the lever flips with the look you already have (no turn, no hotbar change). One right click per press; holding the button does nothing more until you press again. Only your own click: Breaker Aura, Secret Aura and every other automation are unchanged, and Dungeon Breaker's Don't break secrets is not needed for it. With Left Click Etherwarp also on, a left click on a secret opens the secret.
- Starred Mob Hitboxes (named Dungeon ESP on the cheat build) — boxes or glow on starred mobs and secret bats, each with its own color, line-of-sight only; Room Scoped highlights every starred mob in the room you are standing in (plus an adjustable margin for anything that wandered just outside) instead of using a flat range. **Blood Mobs** (off, red by default, its own color) highlights the Watcher's blood mobs the same way - the ones Blood Camp sees land, and any mob whose Hypixel name tag names one of the Watcher's mobs (Mr. Dead, Revoker, Tear, Skull, Walker, Cannibal, Frost, Putrid, Reaper, Vader, Flamer, Freak, Leech, Mute, Ooze, Parasite, Psycho, Wandering Soul, Bonzo, Scarf, Spirit Bear, Livid, and F7's Bigfoot, Jolly Pink Giant, L.A.S.R. and The Diamond Giant), so one that landed while you were away counts too (never the Watcher's Watchful Eyes or your own summons) - kept by identity for as long as they live, so one that walks out of the Blood Room stays highlighted; Range and Room Scoped never limit them. A name tag counts only inside the Blood Room, so Fels and the like elsewhere on the floor are no longer taken for blood mobs. **Fel Skulls** (off, pink by default) highlights dormant Fel skulls - a skull (wither skeleton, plain or player head) on an invisible stand, only those lying within 6 blocks of a starred mob, since starred mobs spawn together; once every starred mob and predicted Fel in a room is dead and the room is still not cleared, its other Fel skulls are highlighted too, until the map shows the room cleared. **Mimic** (off, purple by default) highlights the Mimic (the baby zombie) and, before it comes out, the trapped chest it hides in within 48 blocks. **Hide Ghost Mobs** (off, its own switch, works with every highlight off) hides the "ghost mobs" a teammate leaves when he kills mobs far from you: a dungeon mob (or its health name tag) the server has sent no packet for in 10 seconds while it kept updating everything else is no longer drawn, highlighted, counted by the ESP or targeted by Auto Clear and Auto Routes; the first packet the server sends for it brings it back. A server sends every mob it still tracks for you a position at least every 3 seconds, standing still or not, so a real mob is never hidden; a once-per-dungeon census line in the log records the gaps it saw. A starred mob once seen keeps its highlight for as long as it lives, so one whose star name tag the server stopped sending at range (stands are kept loaded for a shorter distance than mobs) is no longer missed. It tracks them itself, so Blood Camp does not have to be on. In the cheat build the ESP switch draws them through walls like the others
- Wither Boss Highlight — the real F7/M7 wither bosses (Maxor, Storm, Goldor, Necron) at any range with no range limit, as a glow hitbox or a filled hitbox, still line-of-sight only; the cheat build adds a red ESP section with ONE **ESP** switch for everything in it: starred mobs and bats through walls and the wither bosses through walls at any range, in the same wither color as the legit highlight (the ESP has no color of its own); the **Tracer** option (and its thickness) is in the Wither Boss Highlight section on both builds: the legit highlight draws the line only to a wither boss you can see (line of sight), the cheat ESP to every one through walls
- Auto Door Opener — **cheat build only.** Aura or triggerbot on locked wither/blood doors, firing only in a short window after the matching key is obtained rather than continuously. In Triggerbot mode, **Triggerbot Delay** (0-1000 ms, 0 by default) is how long your crosshair must stay on the door before it is clicked. A key message arms it only while it is switched on, and it finds the doors in the dungeon sim too. The old "Look At Door" setting is gone
- Interactive Map — **cheat build only.** The full-screen map: open it on a key, then aim at a room with the cursor and press your Start bind (a key or a mouse button, your choice) to act on it. Pressed in the green room before the run starts (the gate still shut), the trip is planned at once through the gate and waits: nothing moves until the gate opens, and the first etherwarp goes on the tick it does (Dungeon Autopilot's trips wait the same way and never time out while they do). No warp it plans lands on a block without a completely flat, full top face (no bottom slab, low stair, chest, soul sand...; a top slab is fine). A press starts flying the floor graph's plan at once, and while you fly the exact search Auto Routes path nodes use looks for the rest of the trip in fewer warps and swaps it in if you have not reached that warp yet. One press goes there in the fewest etherwarps, and of the landings those reach, the one nearest the middle of the clicked tile rather than just past its doorway - never an extra warp to get further in; a press while a path is already running cancels that path and re-plans to the new room or door instead of being ignored; a press on the room you are standing in runs that room's own secret route; two presses in quick succession path to that room's recorded route START node, so you land ready to secret it (rooms with no recorded route fall back to the room's own spot) - the **Double-Press Secrets** toggle, on by default, turns that off. A separate **Go + Secret Key** (unbound by default; a key or a mouse button) does the same thing on a single press - it goes to the room under the cursor, or the room you are in if the cursor is off the map, and runs its secrets - and works whether or not the double press is on. A press always beats a secret route that is already playing — the route is stopped and the map's goal takes over. Hover for type, secrets, crypts and who cleared it; scroll to zoom. The map never pans: dragging with any mouse button does not move it, and every press acts when you let go, however far the mouse moved - a left press goes (or retargets), and a right or middle press keeps its own action (Locked Door, Go + Secret, or reset the view on an unbound middle button); once the view is zoomed a Reset view line on the side panel puts it back. The side panel shows the room colors two to a row, the door colors, a Controls section that is collapsed to one line (Go) until you click its [+] header (it remembers), and Extra Info (crypts, bat/mimic/prince on one line, S+ secrets on a line of its own when it does not fit beside its label - shown whether or not Score Calculator is on, with a one-line "waiting" note before the run's first numbers); nothing on it overlaps at any window size or Auto Scale factor, and anything that cannot fit is clipped to the panel. Room labels (Off, Checkmarks, Secrets, Room Name, Room Name + Secrets) are ONE setting shared with the Dungeon Map - changing Room Labels in either tab changes both maps - and are drawn exactly as the Dungeon Map draws them, with the same marks (Custom Checkmark Sprite), Font Scale and Text Shadow, but it always draws the whole scanned floor whatever Funny Map says. Paths are searched leg by leg, each leg kept to the two rooms it joins, and a leg with a straight warp into the next doorway is taken without the full search - measured outside the game on a floor of real room captures, a click takes about half a millisecond on average and a few milliseconds at worst; every hop's aim is checked by casting the exact warp it will send, so it lands on the block it was planned for. **Ticks Between Etherwarps** (1-10, 1 by default) sets the pace: a map path sends its next etherwarp that many client ticks after the last, so 1 is a warp every tick. In the dungeon sim the hops chain at that pace, resolved from where the sim's server has you, the way Hypixel resolves them. When the server puts you somewhere the warp plan does not know about (a correction), it says so in chat, plays the Correction Alarm and plans the same goal again from where you are, every time - corrections do not count against its two re-plans. A server lag spike is not a missed warp: while Hypixel's once-a-tick pings are late, the 1-second "Warp N never landed" limit grows by the lag, so it neither warns nor re-plans while the server catches up (the timing with no lag is unchanged). **Trips planned ahead**: whenever the Interactive Map is on in a dungeon - the map screen open or not, with no setting of its own (off with the Interactive Map, and then none of it runs) - as soon as the floor is known and the floor graph can search in milliseconds (about half a second in), the map plans its trip to every room it knows from where you stand - each tile as a press goes there, the room's recorded spot, and its Auto Routes start node - nearest rooms first, then the rooms on the way to Blood and the rooms with routes; it does this again whenever you stand still somewhere new. Then, as rooms load in, it plans from the last node of every room's route to the start node of every other room's route (or to the room itself when it has no route), which Auto Secret and Dungeon Autopilot use for their room order and their next warp. A press whose trip is ready sends its first warp as soon as the server has the sneak, with no search; each trip is exactly the plan a press would have made, and is checked again the moment it is used (every warp cast in the world as it is then, the first one aimed again from exactly where you stand, the last landing where it was planned) - one that no longer holds is dropped and the trip planned fresh at once. A trip planned while only the quick part of the floor graph was ready is never flown once the whole graph is: for the second or so it takes to plan those again, a press there searches (a few milliseconds), so a precomputed trip never takes more warps than a fresh one. A block change drops every trip whose warps pass near it and plans the rest again soon (a door that opened may have made a shorter way). It runs a few milliseconds at a time on the map's own planner thread, giving way to any trip you ask for, and nothing of it runs outside a dungeon or in the boss.
- Terminal Solver — highlights the correct slot(s) to click on Floor 7 terminal puzzles, including Melody detection (with an optional "Send Mel Coords On Open" that types your position into party chat when the Melody terminal opens, and "Send Melody Progress" that posts "Melody 1/3 - 33%" as you finish each row). The **cheat** build additionally has an Auto Terminals mode (with a Min/Max click delay, an input-block safety toggle, and a Melody Skip Mode for how aggressively it clicks ahead: Edges, the default, clicks the Lookahead's extra rows only when the target is the first or last slot of the row and plays a target on any middle slot one row at a time, whatever the Lookahead; All clicks every remaining row after any proper click) that clicks for you - see [Which jar do I download?](#installing) below.
