If a **Confirm Instant Buy** page follows, it clicks the one button whose lore states exactly that amount at a price under the NPC's; anything else stops it. If the sign never opens, Hypixel asks again, or nothing arrives, it stops and says which. Hypixel cuts long menu titles ("Enchanted Pufferfish ➜ Instant"), so the page is recognized by any cut of "&lt;item&gt; ➜ Instant Buy" plus its buttons, and only ever as the product it was buying. Each buy waits for the items to arrive, and "You cannot afford this!" ends the buying. Then it closes the Bazaar, opens `/trades` and shift-clicks every unit to the NPC, waiting for each stack to go, and scans again. Every page is recognized by name, never by slot number, and anything unexpected stops it with the menu's contents in chat. The Bazaar reskin keeps drawing while it runs, showing each page it reaches with "Bazaar Flipper running - Escape stops it" in the bottom bar; the flipper reads and clicks Hypixel's real menu underneath. It needs an active **Booster Cookie** (remote `/bz` and selling from `/trades` both require one) and refuses to start without.
  - *Autopilot* (off by default; the Autopilot row in the tab). The start key then runs a full Bazaar flipping bot instead of the cycle above: it places buy orders and sell offers, uses Insta Buy and Insta Sell where they pay, relists its own orders when they are outbid or expire, collects what filled from Manage Orders, and sells or lists what it collected. What it does follows your `/bzflip` answers (NPC or Bazaar, total or percent, Insta Buy or Buy Order, Insta Sell or Sell Offer) and the Bazaar Advisor's pricing: a buy order bids the best order + 0.1, a sell offer undercuts the cheapest offer by 0.1, sales pay the 1.25% tax, and an order is sized by how much of the product actually trades in a day and how crowded that side of the book is. On Hypixel's "How many do you want?" page it clicks the preset whose lore (or, without an amount line, whose name) states exactly the order's amount - **Buy only one!**, **Buy 4!**, **Buy 32!** and the other **Buy N!** buttons, or the older **Get me a stack!**, **A big stack!** and **One thousand!** - and otherwise **Custom Amount**, typing the number on the sign; Confirm Buy Order must then state that amount or nothing is placed. A new buy order takes at most a third of the purse above the reserve, Insta Buy only takes the sell offers below the sale price, and it insta-sells instead of listing when that nets about as much or the listing would take more than a day. An order outbid after a minute is relisted at the new best price, and canceled instead once the new price no longer pays or after five relists (a price war). It never touches an order that was already there when it started, and never flips an item you already carry (Sell Instantly and a Sell Offer would sell yours too). **Reserve** (typed like Target Purse; empty means a tenth of your starting purse) is never spent and it stops if your purse drops under it; **Max New Orders Per Session** (5, 10, 20, 50 or 100; relists do not count) ends a session once its orders are done. Buy orders are ranked by the profit they can make in a day (the margin times what fills in a day, capped by the order size), never by margin alone, and products with no instant-sell volume in the API are logged and treated as filling nothing. How many buy orders it puts up on one product follows the same daily fills: none under one fill a day, one while a single order (a third of the purse above the reserve, at most 71,680 units) covers the day, and one more for each further order's worth, at most 5; extra orders match the session's own top bid with **Same as Top Order** instead of outbidding it, and a relist matches a sibling order rather than bidding over it. When Hypixel says "[Bazaar] Placing orders is on cooldown for up to N minute(s)!" it pauses for those N minutes from the line's arrival (1 to 5, 1 when no number is given), drops the refused attempt without counting it toward Max New Orders Per Session (a relist's second half is placed after the pause), says so once and carries on. It also stops on any other Bazaar chat line that reads like a refusal or a limit, on any menu it did not expect, and on every safety the cycle has (any key, the click cap, a world change). Target Purse works the same way. With none of its orders open it stops and says why rather than waiting: "nothing on the Bazaar is profitable right now" when it holds nothing, or, selling to the NPC, "the daily NPC sell limit is reached" (what it holds stays with you, and it says when the limit resets); a start while the limit is reached is refused. It never trades on Bazaar prices more than three minutes old by Hypixel's own timestamp: it refreshes them first, and stops naming their age if the refresh fails. Sales go into `/profit bazaarflip`, each also logged as "Sold N item: spent, received, net": spent is what Hypixel's "Bought" line charged, or, when no Bought line came, the Instant Buy page's rounded quote, and the line then says it is an estimate; received is still estimated from the sale price. `tools/bazaarflip-sim` simulates the decisions offline over recorded or synthetic order books.
- Auto Kick — Party Finder only: while you queue, each player who joins your group through Party Finder has their time on the floor you are queuing looked up, and a slower one - or none at all - is kicked (`/p kick`) with a line in chat saying why. Off by default; there is no warn-only mode. The floors are in two groups, Normal Floors (F1-F7) and Master Mode (M1-M7); each floor has its own switch and its own slider, 30 s to 4 min in 5 s steps (Floor 7 to 6 min, Master 7 to 7 min). A floor switched OFF is never judged and nobody is kicked for it; every floor starts ON. **Judge By** picks which time: S+ (the default), S, or S+ else S (S only for a joiner with no S+). **Floor Source** picks the floor: Detect Queued (the default) reads the dungeon type and floor shown on the Group Builder's "Select Dungeon Type" and "Select Floor" items once Hypixel says your party has been queued - Party Finder only, `/joininstance` and `/f7`-style shortcuts are not read - and uses the last run's floor until you have queued anything; Last Run is the floor of the last run you were in; Fixed uses the Fixed Floor setting. Queuing the Entrance leaves no floor, so nobody is judged. It never judges you, never acts while you are in a dungeon, and a lookup that fails kicks nobody. `/autokick populate [player]` (or the "Populate My Times From API" button) sets each floor to that player's (or your own) S+ PB on it + 20 s, rounded to the slider's step (a floor with no PB and the floor switches are left alone).

**AP3 - automated F7/M7 Phase 3 (cheat build only)**

AP3 walks, aligns, leaps, uses items and times you through the F7/M7 Goldor phase (P3, sections S1-S4) along a route of *nodes* you place yourself. It only ever presses real keys - W/A/S/D, sneak, sprint, jump - and turns your real view, exactly like a player would; it never writes your position or velocity. When the server corrects your position it posts a chat line and plays the Correction Alarm, and AP3 carries on from where the server put you (since 2026-10-06; the old Stop On Server Corrections switch and the two-in-ten-seconds auto-disable are gone). Use it on your own risk - it is automation and against Hypixel's rules.

*How it runs*
- Every node has a small trigger box (0.5 or 1 block by default, or any size with w<n> l<n>). Walking INTO a box fires that node; the ones you are already standing in do not fire again until you leave and come back.
- Nodes fire one per tick in a fixed priority: Stopwatch, Stop, Align/Axis Align/Fast Align, Look, Terminal/Blink Charge, Walk/Run, Jump/Edge/Block/Use/Blink Release, Boom, Leap. A queued node waits for the one before it. Look, Stopwatch and Use never wait: they go off on the tick you enter their box, whatever else is running (a Use waits only while something else is aiming you - an align, a Block, a Boom, a Path, a Leap or a Terminal). Walk/Run, Jump and Edge do the same unless a Stop, an align, a Leap or a Path is running or queued ahead of them.
- AP3 knows which section you're in and only arms that section's nodes. Force Dungeon (in the tab) lets you place and test nodes anywhere, and Test Mode runs nodes without waiting for terminals or teammates.
- Freeze View (Freecam): while AP3 turns your real yaw for an align or a walk, your screen keeps the view you had and your mouse moves only the view. Your model in F5 and the server see the real angle.
- Moving yourself (WASD/space) stops AP3 at once - except inside an Align, which takes your keys over until it has landed, and a Boom that is already turning you, whose Superboom still goes out (your keys move you meanwhile) before AP3 stops.
- Another feature that turns your body for a click (Lights Device, Lever Aura, Secret Aura and the rest) waits while an Align runs or a Boom, Block, Use or Look is aiming you. During a Walk/Run it may only turn you to an angle where W, W+A or W+D still runs exactly along the walk (any up/down angle that reaches the lever), so your path and sprint do not change; when no such angle reaches it yet, the click waits for one.

*Placing nodes* - `/ap3 add <type> [modifiers]`, or a keybind for each type
- Nodes snap to the nearest half block (a block center, or the line between two or four blocks) unless you add `precise`; the height is your exact feet height, so nodes on carpet or slabs sit on top.
- The angle recorded is where you are looking (or where your free camera looks while Freeze State is on). Only the nodes that use an angle keep it: Walk and Run keep the yaw, Look, Boom, Block and Use keep yaw and pitch, and every other node is saved without one.
- `/ap3 edit <n>` opens the node editor: position, box, wait, leap counter, jump/edge and the fields that node's type actually uses. The type is a dropdown; picking another type adds or removes rows on the spot (switching an Align to a Look adds Yaw and Pitch, starting at your current view). A Use node's item is a dropdown of your hotbar - click the item to use, or "Held item / none"; an item saved earlier that is no longer on the bar still shows. Nothing is applied until Save. A yaw or pitch is kept as you typed it (167.9 stays 167.9, it no longer comes back as 167.899994).
- `/ap3 list`, `/ap3 undo`, `/ap3 redo`, `/ap3 delete [n]`, `/ap3 replace <n>`, `/ap3 clear`, `/ap3 reload`, `/ap3 stop`, `/ap3 testmode`. Routes are saved per config file in the killer560smod-ap3 folder; Choose AP3 Config switches between them. `/ap3 undo` now takes back the last add, delete, move, re-place, editor change or clear, whatever area it was in and as many steps back as you like (until the game closes or `/ap3 reload`) - it used to remember only the last added node; with nothing left it still deletes the last node of the chain you stand in. `/ap3 redo` (and its keybind) re-applies what undo took back; any new change clears the redo history. Sliders in the AP3 tab are not undo steps.

*Node types*
- **Align** - lands you on the node's point to within 0.001 of a block, typically in 6-9 ticks from entering at speed. It plans the fastest landing each tick, mixing turning your real yaw, every key combination and sneak taps, and only counts as done once you have actually stopped. Every align (Fast Align too) checks the floor under where each press will put you: a press that would take you off a ledge more than half a block high gets Sneak held, so the game itself stops you at the edge, and a point placed past such a drop is aligned to as close as is safe and then left, with a chat line saying so.
- **Axis Align** - for a node placed against a wall: walks you into the wall (which pins that coordinate exactly), then holds one key into the wall at a small angle that steers you along it until you stop exactly on the point, without ever pulling off the wall.
- **Fast Align** (`/ap3 add fastalign`) - an align built only for speed: each tick it plans the fewest ticks until you are standing still on the point (within 0.001), combining a camera turn to any angle with any key combo and sneak, so it brakes with a sneaking tap against your momentum instead of coasting to a stop. In testing it finishes in 3 ticks about 83% of the time and never takes more than 4, where the regular Align takes 8-11 (up to 16).
- **Path** (`/ap3 add path [start|end] [<step>]`) - the route optimizer. Each Path node is a numbered step: `/ap3 add path 2` or `/ap3 add path two`, and with no number it takes the lowest one still free (place 1 and 3, the next one becomes 2). Nodes that SHARE a number are one step to be done in any order - put both levers on step 2 and it works out whether to take them one after the other or, if their boxes overlap, run between them and take both on the same tick. Place Path nodes in order and it works out the fastest way to hit every one of them, from the first to the last, without skipping any: it searches the real movement model a tick at a time (key combination, yaw and jump) and plays the answer back through the same key-writing every other node uses. Modifiers: `exact` (keep the block relationship you placed it with - the hitbox covers the same blocks, on or off an edge - rather than a meaningless decimal match), `speed:<min>-<max>` (the speed window it must cross at, blocks per tick; `speed:0.4-` at least, `speed:-0.3` at most), `dir:<deg>` with `dirtol:<deg>` (the heading it must be traveling at), and `term` (stop here, wait for a terminal to be closed, and plan the next leg from rest - a term costs you all your speed, so there is no point arriving fast). A Block node's block is understood too: the spot it will place is treated as floor for two ticks of running (a bottom slab at most), and the route never places it itself. Whether it jumps is worked out, not assumed: the air only accelerates you at a flat 0.026 no matter your speed stat, so bunny-hopping wins at vanilla speed and loses badly at 550. The world is read into a snapshot of its real collision boxes each time it plans, and every tick of the search sweeps your 0.6x1.8 hitbox against them the way the game does - one axis at a time, with the same step-up search - so stairs, slabs and ledges are planned on what actually happens rather than on a height profile. That is what lets a route run up a staircase: it climbs half a block a tick and knows that each riser takes your speed with it, so it plans around the climb instead of expecting to keep sprinting through it. Blocks Breaker Aura is going to break count as air, lava is somewhere the route may not go, and the plan is drawn through the world at the heights it will really run at. Mark the two ends of a route with `start` and `end`: one section can then hold several separate routes and each knows where it stops, instead of running on into the next one. To extend a route, delete its `end` node and place a new one further along. A chain with no `end` marked anywhere behaves as it always did and runs to the last Path node in it. Each way of arriving at a route is searched ONCE. The first time you run it from a given spot the search is allowed to think hard - seconds, a wide beam, several differently-shaped searches, and a longer second attempt if the first ran out of clock - and the answer goes in the config folder; arrive that way again and it loads instantly and runs exactly the same, because a saved plan is never replaced once it exists. Arrive somewhere quite different and it searches once more and remembers that approach too. What it will not do is hand back a plan built somewhere else: a plan is a fixed schedule of key presses, and replayed from the wrong place it does not go to the node, it repeats the movements that went to the node from another one. `/ap3 regenerate` forgets the plans for the route you are standing in, so the next run searches again. When a route is too tangled to plan in one piece - a gap, then a ledge, then a climb - it is cut up and planned a piece at a time, landing on each cut point, because a route that pauses is worth more than a route that does not exist. A route that never leaves the ground always wins over one that jumps, however the tick counts compare, and running into the world costs the search real ticks so it will coast, walk or take off early to keep a clean line - though a collision that gains height is how you climb a step and is charged nothing.
- **No Go** (`/ap3 add nogo`) - a GHOST BLOCK: solid to the route planner, absent from the world. It never fires as a node and nothing is ever placed or sent; it simply tells the route there is a block here even though there is not, so the route can stand on it, step up it, be stopped by it and jump off it. Use it to build the geometry a movement needs when the room does not provide it. A full block on the node's own level, sized by the node's box.
- **Term Aura** (`/ap3 add termaura`) - steps on it and clicks a terminal in range once to open it, trying again two ticks later if nothing opened. A terminal your look misses gets your body - never your camera - turned to it first (Terminal Aura's Turn To Terminal setting), and the chain carries on under the terminal it opened, so a Terminal node after it takes over. It does not turn the Terminal Aura setting on or off - it is one pulse of it. It also stops you exactly like a Stop: it ends a Walk/Run, lets go of every key and waits until you've stopped (the click goes out meanwhile), and like a Stop it fires once each time you step into it.
- **Walk / Run** - keeps you walking (or sprinting) in the direction the node was placed, until another Walk/Run (which takes over), a Stop or an align (which end it), a Leap (which drops all movement) or a Path (which takes the movement over). Every other node fires with the walk still going; while a screen a node opened is up (a terminal) the walk presses nothing and carries on when it closes. A Stop or align hit while another node is still busy ends the walk at once. Your real yaw snaps so W+A / W+D (45 Degree Strafe on, the faster diagonal) or W points exactly along the walk from the first tick. On the tick a jump goes in it faces straight ahead so the sprint-jump boost follows the walk, then strafes again in the air. Since 2026-10-27 it never walks you off a drop more than half a block below where it started unless the route's next node is lower or the route jumps there (a Jump/Edge node or modifier): it stops early enough to come to rest at the edge, and brakes. A run that passes within 2.5 blocks of the route's next align without entering its box begins that align (it pulls you back onto it).
- **Jump** - jumps on the next tick after you enter it (waiting up to a second for the ground). Does not end a Walk/Run.
- **Edge Jump** (`edge`) - jumps at the edge when you run off a block: on the one-tick "coyote" window just after your feet leave it, or one tick sooner when that tick is nearer the edge (at a full run the coyote tick can be most of a block past it). Does not end a Walk/Run.
- **Block** - places a block (the held block, else a slab, else any block in your hotbar) where you were looking when you placed the node, then swaps back to what you held. The block shows on your screen at once, so you can run across ghost slabs. Does not end a Walk/Run. Like Use, it places on the tick you enter its box: the swap and the place are the first things sent after the movement packet that put you there, and the swap back goes out once, the tick after.
- **Look** - turns your view to the node's angle on the tick you enter its box - the next frame already shows it - client-side only (no rotation is sent). During a walk only the view turns (the walk keeps its own heading underneath, as with Freeze View); when the walk ends your real rotation glides onto the look.
- **Use** - right-clicks along the node's angle on the tick you enter its box: the click is the first thing sent after the movement packet that put you in the box, swapping first to the item you held when you placed it if it is elsewhere in your hotbar (slot change and click in the same tick, like a hotbar key and a right click). The click carries the node's angle and the next movement packet reports the same angle, as a real client's does. A block in reach is clicked (a lever, a chest), otherwise the item is used into the air. Does not end a Walk/Run.
- **Boom** - uses a Superboom on the block the node's angle meets from where you placed it, on the tick you enter its box (swap and tap sent first thing after the movement packet that put you there), turning you to that block from wherever in the box you are - the edge at sprint speed, or above it while you fall in. If the block is not in reach yet it keeps trying for a second instead of giving up. Once it has started it runs to its tap: a Look reached meanwhile waits for it, other features cannot turn you, and leaving its box does not cancel it. Does not end a Walk/Run.
- **Leap** - opens Spirit Leap and leaps to a class, a player, or your Fast Leap target. (The Leap Counter is now the `leapcounter:<n>` modifier below; `/ap3 add leapcounter <n>` and old routes give a Stop carrying it.)
- **Terminal** - waits for the terminal you're at to be done (a Walk/Run carries on - put a Stop before it to stand at the terminal; nothing moves while the terminal is open). **Stop** - lets go of every key and waits until you've stopped. It fires once each time you step into it: while you stay inside its box it does not fire again or stop the nodes that come after it, and walking out and back in arms it again.
- **Blink Charge** (`/ap3 add blinkcharge [ticks] [stop]`) - charges Blink on the node until that many ticks are banked (1-20; with no number, Blink's Charge Ticks setting), then the route moves on with the bank kept. Your momentum is frozen while it charges: every banked tick sends nothing, so no game time passes for you on the server, and your own physics pauses to match (no gravity, no friction, nothing written) - when the charge is done you carry on at exactly the speed and in the direction you came in with, on the ground or in mid-jump, as if the charge had never happened. With `stop` (or Momentum: stop first in the node editor) it is a plain stop instead: your walk ends, you brake, then bank standing. It drives Blink's Charge itself, so your Charge key does not need to be bound and does nothing to it meanwhile. If AP3 stops (a leap, a death, a screen, you moving), the charge and its bank are handed back to your Charge key with the node's cap: a later Blink Release node, your key, or switching Charge off fires or ends it; only turning AP3 off ends it. Blink must be on: with Blink off the node says so once in chat and the route waits on it (turn Blink on and it carries on). Like any wait, a left click skips it.
- **Blink Release** (`/ap3 add blinkrelease [ticks]`) - fires that many banked ticks as a Blink shot (with no number, everything banked). AP3 steers every tick of the shot exactly as it would in real time: the nodes you reach inside it fire, Runs turn you, aligns, jumps, stops, the ledge and hazard guards all act per shot tick, so a release follows your route instead of running straight on. A shot ends early, keeping the rest banked, at a node that needs the server or the real clock (a Use, Block, Boom or Term Aura sending - the shot carries on once that node is done -, a Leap, Terminal, Leap Counter or leapcounter: wait, another Blink Charge, a Path, a wait: modifier), when a screen opens, when AP3 stops (the rest stays banked and goes back to your Charge key), or once you have come to rest. What is not fired stays banked, so one charge can be split over several releases (`blinkcharge 10`, then `blinkrelease 4` and `blinkrelease 6`). It keeps a shot's rules (at most 9.5 blocks a tick, the auras act in every shot tick). With nothing banked it fires nothing and says why in the log. When the bank is spent the charge ends. Does not end a Walk/Run.
- Both take every modifier below and have a Blink ticks box in the node editor (blank = the max).
- **Crystal** (`/ap3 add crystal [height]`) - for getting crystals with Spring Boots: when you reach it your walk ends and it holds crouch, charging the boots, until the charge is high enough, then lets go of the crouch so the jump goes. The height is the Spring Boots HUD's count (the charge notes Hypixel plays). With a number (`/ap3 add crystal 30`, or `h:30`, up to 61 blocks, or the node editor's Height box) it charges to that; with none it charges to whatever puts you at the nearest end crystal above the node (within 6 blocks sideways), and with no crystal there to the full charge. If no charge note is heard within 2 seconds (no Spring Boots on) AP3 stops and says why. It never crouches longer than 10 seconds.
- **Stopwatch** - the first one starts a timer, the next one stops it and prints the time. Name the starting one (`/ap3 add stopwatch s3`) and, with Send Stopwatch to Party on, the time goes to party chat as "s3 took 12.345s". The Stopwatch HUD shows the time while it runs and for 10 seconds after it stops, then hides until the next start (the HUD editor always previews it). A stopwatch fires in the very tick you touch its box (running onto the end stopwatch included), except that while an align (Align, Axis Align, Fast Align) is working on the same block it waits and fires in the tick the align finishes.

*Modifiers* (any node, after the type)
- `w<n> l<n>` (also `w.5`, `.5x.5`) - the trigger box size. `precise` - no snapping. `wait:<ms>` - hold the next node that long after this one. (The old `close` modifier is gone; chains saved with it still load and the node simply fires when you walk into it.)
- `jump` / `edge` - after the node has done its part, jump (or edge-jump). `/ap3 add run edge` runs at the node's angle and jumps at the edge.
- `sectiondone:<1-4>` (also `sd:<n>`) - the node goes only once that P3 section is complete this run (all its terminals, levers and its device, Hypixel's "(n/n)" line), wherever you are standing: wait at a safe spot in S4 with `sectiondone:3` and it goes the tick S3 ends. A section already done when you step on it goes at once. Read from Hypixel's own objective lines with the real-name rule, so another player's chat cannot set it off; a new run (Goldor's line, a new world) starts over. A left click skips the wait. Also "Section done" in the node editor.
- `crystals:<1|2>` (also `cw:<n>`) - the node goes only once that many End Crystals appear within 5 blocks of you after the wait began (an Energy Crystal you place on a Maxor pad): wait with `crystals:2` and it goes the tick the second one stands. Crystals already standing do not count, and neither does one farther than 5 blocks (3D). A left click skips the wait. Also "Wait For Crystal" (Off / 1 / 2) in the node editor; old route files load as Off.
- Blink Charge + Blink Release + Run in the same block (any order you placed them): you freeze the moment you step on it, keep your momentum while the bank fills, and the tick it is full the release fires along the Run (since 2026-10-27; before, one ordinary tick went by first).
- `close` - the node goes only after a terminal is closed: walk in, do the terminal, close it, and it fires (a terminal you finished while standing in its box before its turn counts too). Until then nothing fires except aligns; a left click skips the wait. Also "After terminal" in the node editor.
- Every wait (`leapcounter:`, `close`, `sectiondone:`) takes one left click to skip, every time: opening a screen (chat, the leap menu, a terminal) while a node waits no longer drops the wait, and if anything else stops AP3 while you stand in a waiting node's box, one left click there still fires it (since 2026-10-27; before, you had to walk off and back on).
- `leapcounter:<n>` (also `lc <n>`, 1-4) - when the node is reached it first waits until n teammates have leapt to you, and until then nothing fires except aligns: your walk ends, an Align you reach still lands you, and every other node waits its turn and runs in order once the count is in. Also `/ap3 set <n> leapcounter <k>` (0 removes it) and the Leap counter box in the node editor.
- `ss` - the node fires only once Simon Says is done this run. Walk into it before then and it waits ("waits for Simon Says"), then fires the tick the Simon Says device completes, if you are still in its box; after that it fires as soon as you walk in. Also "After SS" in the node editor.
- Two Walk/Run boxes that overlap: the one you are more in the center of is the one that goes. Entering a second Walk/Run while you are still nearer the middle of the first does nothing yet; it takes over the tick you are more central in it.

*Display*
- Node boxes with arrows (walk direction), wall markers (axis align) and look rays; labels with number, type and details; a color per node type (or one color for all); Show Nodes and Show Node Lines to hide them; Node Messages (None / Simple "Added #3 Walk" / Detailed, which also shows the queue).

*Freeze State* (its own red tab; never use it on Hypixel)
- The Freeze State key freezes your character in place (no movement, no gravity) while your camera stays free - handy for placing nodes at an exact angle.
- Rewind 1 Tick / Forward 1 Tick keys: rewind puts you exactly where you were one tick before your current position (Rewind Memory sets how many ticks are kept); forward predicts - the game runs exactly one real tick from where you are.
- AP3 keeps running while frozen: each forward step runs one AP3 tick, and your own keys and mouse never move the character - only the nodes do.
- On Hypixel the step keys refuse (it would teleport you - an instant ban). There is no command (`/freezestate` and `/rewind` were removed on 2026-11-01).

**Mining** (the Mining category, all off by default but Resume Held Break After Menus, Powder Ghast and the Crystal Hollows powder grinding features below; the Crystal Hollows Map and the features after it came from the staging-mining-3 branch on 2026-11-04)
- Commission HUD — your commissions from the tab list's Commissions widget on a movable HUD (Color By Progress: red under 25%, gold under 75%, yellow above, green when DONE). Live Progress (on by default) moves a mithril or titanium commission on with every counted block instead of waiting for the tab list's slow refresh: the block must be the one you were mining and the server must turn it to bedrock or air, and an area commission counts only blocks inside its area (from the area map Mithril As Stone uses). One block is 1/350 of Mithril Miner, 1/250 of an area's Mithril, 1/15 of Titanium Miner and 1/10 of an area's Titanium (the wiki's Commissions table); when the tab shows that you get more or less per block (Compact, Efficient Miner, starter commissions) the step is learned from it. Blocks Mining Spread breaks beside the one you dug in the same moment count too. The tab still decides: a reading ahead moves it up, and when the shown value drifts more than about three blocks past what the tab will soon catch up to, it resyncs to the tab at once; nothing reads 100% before DONE. While the King's or an Emissary's Commissions menu is open, the HUD also reads each commission item (its name and the percent on its Progress line, or COMPLETED / Click to claim as DONE) and shows that over an older tab reading until the tab catches up to it, shows a newer state, or 30 seconds pass; the prediction resyncs to the menu's numbers. Each tab refresh of a counted commission logs one line (predicted, tab, blocks counted) for tuning. Gemstone, Glacite, Tungsten and Umber collectors and mob commissions stay on the tab's number. Commission Waypoints puts a label with its distance in the world where each open commission is done: the Dwarven Mines area for an area commission ("Lava Springs Mithril" at Lava Springs, a titanium commission in titanium's color) and the Glacite Tunnels gemstone spots for a gemstone Collector commission. Emissary Waypoints labels every Emissary while any commission reads DONE, so you can turn it in at the nearest; each label is the name with its distance in gray. Completed Commissions (on by default) puts a green background behind every finished commission in the King's or an Emissary's Commissions menu (its item says COMPLETED, Click to claim or 100%); it only draws and never clicks. Commission Mobs (off by default) boxes the mobs that count for your open commissions, found by their name tags: goblins (Goblin, Weakling, Knifethrower, Fireslinger) for Goblin Slayer, Glacite Walkers, Treasure Hoarders, Star Sentries, and in the Crystal Hollows Automatons, Sludges, Team Treasurite members, Yogs, Thysts and Boss Corleone; a DONE commission boxes nothing. A mob is boxed only while you can see it, in Commission Mob Color, at any distance: once recognized by its name tag a mob stays highlighted for as long as it is loaded, even after Hypixel stops sending the name tag at range (no mob is matched by its entity type alone, so a far mob whose tag never arrived is not guessed). Commission Mob Style draws it as an Outline (default), a Fill, Fill + Outline, or a Glow (the same glow outline Dungeon ESP uses); the cheat build's Commission Mobs Through Walls draws every style through blocks. Hide While Powder Mining (on by default) hides the HUD, the waypoints, the mob boxes and the Commission Route line while you are in the Crystal Hollows or within two minutes of a treasure chest being uncovered or looted there. Needs the Commissions widget in your tab list; with no widget nothing is shown or guessed.
- Pickobulus Estimate — while you hold a tool whose ability is Pickobulus and it is ready, outlines every block it would break where you aim: it lands on the first block within 20 blocks of your sight line and breaks the exposed blocks within 3 of it; in the Dwarven Mines, Gold Mine and Deep Caverns only ore and mineable blocks, in the Crystal Hollows and Mineshafts everything, in the Glacite Tunnels the tunnel ores. Ready comes from Hypixel's own chat lines first: the outlines go the moment you use it ("You used your Pickobulus Pickaxe Ability!", "Your Pickobulus destroyed N blocks!", "This ability is on cooldown") and come back the moment "Pickobulus is now available!" arrives; a use holds until its ready line (at most 2 minutes), a ready line for 15 seconds, and only then does the tab list's Pickaxe Ability widget, which Hypixel refreshes slowly, decide. Outline Color.
- Ability Ready Alert — a big "<ability> READY" popup (movable, 2.5 s) when Hypixel says a pickaxe ability "is now available!", with an optional Ready Sound. Cooldown Timer adds a movable HUD line per ability still cooling down, timed from the tab list's Pickaxe Ability widget, Hypixel's "This ability is on cooldown for Ns." reply, or the cooldown measured this session between "You used your X Pickaxe Ability!" and its ready line. The widget only refreshes every few seconds, so the line counts down every second from the last reading and moves only when a new reading disagrees by more than 1.5 s (it never jumps back up); the use line starts the measured cooldown at once and the ready line ends it, whatever the tab still says.
- Commission Route — its own tab and switch (off by default, not tied to the Commission HUD). Draws a line in Route Color from your crosshair (half a block along your look, like the other tracers) along the Dwarven Mines' paths (SkyHanni's island graph, the one the area map uses) to the area of your first open area commission ("Lava Springs Mithril" leads to Lava Springs; a commission with no area, such as Mithril Miner or Goblin Slayer, is skipped for the next one that has an area); for a Mithril or Titanium area commission the line goes to a spot in that area that actually has ore (a node of the path graph with at least 6 exposed ore blocks within 7 blocks, counted from the loaded world and refreshed every few seconds, faster near you, so mined-out veins stop being the goal), not just the area's edge, and it goes away when you are at that spot, not merely in the area; where no ore spot is known (chunks not loaded) or for another kind of commission, it goes to the area and goes away once you are in it. Route Mode picks how you get there. Walk is the walking path. Etherwarp and AOTE search the walking graph plus warp hops, so a spot a warp reaches directly (up through the air, across a gap) is one hop, not the way round: Etherwarp hops to any path point you can etherwarp onto from where you stand (57 blocks, solid with two free blocks above, a face in sight) and costs each warp like 25 blocks of walking, so the fewest warps win; AOTE hops to the path point an 8-block teleport along your look lands on (it stops at blocks) and costs each like 5 blocks. Each landing is highlighted as a 3x3 of whole blocks around it: in each of the nine columns the highest block you could etherwarp onto within three below to two above the landing is boxed (a column with none is left out), with the landing block itself outlined brighter. The plan holds still: it is rebuilt only when the target, mode or map changes or you walk more than 8 blocks off it, and points and hops you reach simply drop off the front of the line. Claiming: a claim is due while the tab reads DONE, and from the chat line "... Commission Complete! Visit the King to claim your rewards!" until the tab's list next updates (10 seconds at most); it ends when the tab shows no DONE or you close the Commissions menu. While one is due, Route To Emissary (on by default) sends the line to the nearest Emissary (it goes away within 6 blocks of it; off, the normal area line carries on), and Highlight Royal Pigeon (on by default, its own switch, works even with the Commission Route switch off) pulses the Royal Pigeon in your hotbar if you have one. The two are independent; both off does nothing at a claim. It only draws; nothing is aimed, clicked or sent. Needs the Commissions widget in your tab list; with no widget nothing is shown or guessed.
- Swap To Drill On Pigeon (cheat only, off by default) - when you use the Royal Pigeon, the held hotbar slot changes to a drill right away, before the Commissions menu opens, so the claim is made with that drill in hand. Drill Choice: Auto (Gemstone Powder in the Crystal Hollows, Mithril Powder anywhere else), Mithril Powder, Gemstone Powder, or Specific Drill (a cycling button over the drills in your hotbar; any part of the drill's name or Skyblock id matches). Drills are read from the hotbar (slots 1-8 only, never slot 9) by Skyblock id (DRILL or GAUNTLET) and ranked by their lore: the highest Compact level first, then the largest number on a line naming the chosen powder, then the lowest slot. Note the wiki says the powder of a claim comes from the commission's area, your Heart of the Mountain level and daily status, not from the held item; the held-item effect it mentions is Compact (more Mining XP on a claim), which is what Auto ranks first. It only changes the selected slot: no items moved, no clicks. It waits one tick if the slot change would follow the use packet inside the same server tick, and does nothing when the menu is already open, no drill is in the hotbar (a short chat line says so), or the setting is off. Chat: one short line when it swaps.
- Powder Chest Triggerbot (cheat only, off by default) - while your crosshair is on a Crystal Hollows treasure chest you uncovered (the ones Treasure Chest Highlight boxes), it right-clicks that spot for you, so the locks are picked as you look at them. Once the lock's critical particles are seen (as Lockpick Helper reads them), only a crosshair on that lock's side is clicked. Min CPS and Max CPS (1-20, default 10 each; 20 is a click every game tick; Min never goes above Max) set how fast it clicks - every click picks a new random rate between them - and it never clicks one chest twice in one tick. It forgets its chests on a world change, and never clicks with a menu open or outside the Crystal Hollows. Swap To Best Gemstone Drill (on by default): before the click, the held hotbar slot (1-8, never 9) changes to the drill with the highest Gemstone Powder bonus on its lore - the wiki's chest loot modifiers are Ruby Drill TX-15 +5%, Gemstone Drill LT-522 +10%, Topaz Drill KGR-12 +15% and Jasper Drill X +20% - or, with none of those, any drill; the drill you hold wins a tie. The slot change goes one tick and the click the next. Swap Back When Mining (on by default, shown while the swap is on): after that swap, the first time you start mining a block that is not a chest the held slot goes back to the one you held before, unless that slot no longer holds the same item. Don't Swap While Mining (on by default): no swap to the drill while your attack button is held. No chat lines.
- Wrong Armor Alert — off by default. While you are mining - on a mining island (Dwarven Mines, Glacite Tunnels, Crystal Hollows, Gold Mine, Deep Caverns or a Glacite Mineshaft), holding a mining tool (a pickaxe, drill or gauntlet: its Skyblock id, or the "Breaking Power" line every mining tool has) and actually breaking blocks (the attack button held on a block in the last 3 seconds) - a big red "Wrong Armor!" line (movable in the HUD editor) shows whenever any armor slot holds a piece that is not part of a mining set, or is empty; it goes half a second after that stops being true. Mixing pieces of two mining sets is fine. Alert Sound (on by default) is an alarm, a two-tone beep repeated every second for as long as the armor stays wrong. The sets, matched by Skyblock item id (from each set's page on hypixelskyblock.minecraft.wiki, 2026-11-03): Miner Armor (TANK_MINER_*), Lapis Armor (LAPIS_ARMOR_*), Prospecting Armor (MINER_OUTFIT_*), Heat Armor (HEAT_*), Goblin Armor (GOBLIN_*), Glacite Armor (GLACITE_*), Flamebreaker Armor (FLAME_BREAKER_*), Ember Ash Armor (FLAMEBREAKER_*), Yog Armor (ARMOR_OF_YOG_*), Sorrow Armor (SORROW_*), Armor of Divan (DIVAN_*), Mineral Armor (MINERAL_*) and Glossy Mineral Armor (GLOSSY_MINERAL_*), each with _HELMET, _CHESTPLATE, _LEGGINGS and _BOOTS. The Fallen Star Helmet does not count (the wiki lists no mining stat on it).
- Jungle Pickaxe Alert — off by default, in the Wrong Armor Alert's card (Mining, General) with its own switch (the card is ON while either alert is). In the Crystal Hollows only, while you hold the Jungle Pickaxe (Skyblock id JUNGLE_PICKAXE) and break blocks (the attack button held on a block in the last 3 seconds) outside the Jungle zone (the low X, low Z surface quadrant above the Magma Fields layer, the same lines Crystal Hollows Walls draws, minus the Nucleus box), a big red "Jungle Pickaxe Outside The Jungle!" line (movable in the HUD editor) shows; it goes half a second after that stops being true. The pickaxe is for the Jungle Temple, so using it elsewhere is a mistake. Jungle Alert Sound (on by default) plays one two-tone beep when the alert appears, once per mining stretch.
- Carpet Highlighter — in the Dwarven Mines, fills the unbreakable carpets in ore veins (a gray, light blue or light gray carpet on a Sea Lantern) by drawing each one with the red carpet's texture (the chunk mesher swaps the model; the world is unchanged), so differently colored carpets all read the same and you stop swinging at them. Carpets (and titanium) follow the server's block changes the tick they arrive, and new chunks the tick they load.
- Mithril As Stone — in the Dwarven Mines, draws mithril (gray and light blue wool, the prismarines, cyan terracotta) as stone unless an open commission wants mithril or titanium in the area that block is in, wherever you stand: with Upper Mines Mithril open, the Upper Mines' mithril shows and Cliffside Veins' turns to stone even while you stand in Cliffside Veins. A block's area comes from SkyHanni's Dwarven Mines map (the same download pathfinding uses: each block takes the area of the nearest map point within 24 blocks, and a block with none that near the nearest one that names an area within 64); the area you stand in never decides a block once the map is loaded, so looking out from one area, the blocks of another keep their own answer. Without the map (not downloaded yet) a block takes the area you are in. Anywhere: a commission with no area in its name (Mithril Miner, Titanium Miner, as the HOTM perk that lets them count everywhere gives them) applies everywhere by itself. Render only: the block is still mithril to the game and to the server. Needs the Commissions widget in your tab list.
- Dark Stone — on the mining islands, re-skins the textures you already have loaded (so it follows your resource pack) darker: stone, cobblestone, andesite, diorite, granite and their polished forms, deepslate, tuff, packed and blue ice, and clay. Ores (coal, iron, gold, lapis, redstone, emerald, diamond, copper, plain and deepslate) get only the pixels that belong to the stone behind them darkened, exactly like the stone around them, and keep their colored specks. Gemstones and Titanium (polished diorite) are untouched. Darkness 10-90% (default 50), Darken Mithril Too (off). It only redraws the block atlas when you switch, change the level or enter or leave a mining island; nothing runs per frame or per vertex. Held and inventory blocks use the same textures, so they are darker too while it is active. Legit, both builds.
- Titanium Highlight — outlines Titanium (polished diorite) in the Dwarven Mines: the whole box of every block in your line of sight, nothing for one hidden behind other blocks. Commission Area Only: on, only the titanium in an area a commission wants (by the area the block is in, as for Mithril As Stone, or every block with a Titanium Miner that counts anywhere); off, everywhere. Titanium Color. Cheat build: Through Walls, with Exposed Only (on by default: only titanium with a face open to air, so you can reach and mine it).
- Powder Ghast — **off by default**. In the Dwarven Mines, when Hypixel says the Powder Ghast has spawned ("The sound of pickaxes clashing against the rock has attracted the attention of the POWDER GHAST!"), a POWDER GHAST title and a sound; the place from "Find the Powder Ghast near the ...!" becomes the subtitle. Only Hypixel's own lines count, never a player's message. Despawn Timer: a movable HUD line counting down its minute (each hit resets it, as on Hypixel), or "Powder Ghast alive" when it is seen without the spawn line. Highlight Box (Box Color) outlines the ghast while you can see it, and Tracer (Tracer Color) draws a line from your crosshair to it while it is loaded. Party Callout (off by default) sends "Powder Ghast near the <place>!" to party chat once per spawn. Cheat build: Through Walls draws the box through blocks. It only draws; nothing is aimed or clicked.
- Raffle Ticket Highlight — during the Dwarven Mines Raffle, boxes each raffle ticket on the ground (a dropped Name Tag or an item named Raffle Ticket). Ticket Color. Cheat build: Through Walls.
- Resume Held Break After Menus — **off by default**. On a mining island, when a menu closes while you are still holding left click (a menu the server opens mid-dig, such as the Commissions menu after a commission completes, lets go of the button for the game), you go on breaking without pressing it again: the game is handed the press your held button made (when you aim at a block), so it starts digging that block exactly as if you had pressed again. Let go during the menu and nothing happens. It sends only what a fresh press on that block sends (the dig start and a swing); aimed at a mob, it only holds the key down, as a held button would. The log's "[AttackHold]" lines name each screen that opened under a held click, and for a container the last packets the game sent and the last chat and title lines before it (command names only, never chat text).
- Golden Goblin — off by default. On a mining island, draws a box and a tracer (from the crosshair) on the Golden Goblin and the Diamond Goblin, found by their name tags (whole words; a real player is never one) and remembered while loaded. The box and the tracer take the goblin's own color: Golden Color (gold) for the Golden Goblin and Diamond Color (aqua) for the Diamond Goblin, both settings. Only a goblin in plain sight is drawn; the cheat build's Through Walls row (cheat block at the bottom) draws them through blocks. Draws only, sends nothing.
- Treasure Chest Highlight — **off by default**. In the Crystal Hollows, when Hypixel says "You uncovered a treasure chest!" and a chest appears within 10 blocks of you (in either order, within 3 seconds), that chest gets a box (Box Color), depth-tested so walls hide it. Fade to Red (on) turns the box red as its minute runs out. Despawn Timer (on): a movable HUD line, "Treasure Chest: 42s" (the count when there are several), for the chest that runs out first. Only your own chests are boxed: any other chest, and a player's copy of the line, are ignored. A chest leaves when its lid opens, when Hypixel's loot message or "This chest has already been looted." answers clicks that were all on that one chest in the last 1.5 seconds (a click the server ignored, or a second chest clicked before the answer came, takes no highlight off any chest: it stays boxed and the Powder Chest Triggerbot keeps clicking it), when its block is gone, after its minute, or when you change worlds. Cheat build: Through Walls draws the box through blocks. It only draws. Extra rows (Hide Opened Chests, Countdown Over Chests, Tracer, Tracer Mode): Hide Opened Chests (off by default) stops drawing a claimed chest's model once the server confirms it opened (its lid opens, its loot or "already looted" line names it, or its block goes after your click; a click the server ignores hides nothing) (client-only render skip; the block is never changed, so no ghost block), and the spot stays hidden for 5 seconds after the chest block goes, so a chest the server sends again while it removes it never flickers back; Countdown Over Chests (on) shows the seconds left above each claimed chest, green with the whole minute left, yellow at half, red near the end; Tracer (off) draws lines to the claimed chests, each line in the color of the chest it goes to (its box color, Fade to Red included); Tracer Mode (shown while Tracer is on, left click forward, right click back) is Chain (default: crosshair to the oldest chest, then that chest to the next oldest, and so on) or From Crosshair (one line from the crosshair to each chest). The lines are depth-tested in the legit build and through walls with the cheat build's Through Walls.
- Treasure Chest Alert — **off by default**. A sound (on) and an optional TREASURE CHEST title (off) the moment Hypixel says "You uncovered a treasure chest!" in the Crystal Hollows.
- Powder Break Sounds — **off by default**. While powder mining (in the Crystal Hollows, or within 2 minutes of a treasure chest), every block break and digging hit sound within 6 blocks of you (hardstone, gemstones, stone; your own mining included, since Hypixel breaks the block on its side and sends the sound) is changed. Mode (left click forward, right click back): Mute silences breaks and hits (the break particles stay) or Custom silences the hits and plays the Sound you pick for each break (Experience Orb, Note Pling, Harp, Bell, Chime, Amethyst Chime, Level Up, Arrow Hit, Item Pickup, Villager Yes, Anvil Land, Blaze Hurt) at the Volume (0.1 to 2.0) and Pitch (0.5 to 2.0) you set, with a Test button. OFF is the normal break sound. Breaks farther than 6 blocks away are untouched. Client-side only, both builds.
- Crystal Hollows Walls — legit, off by default (Mining, with the powder tabs). In the Crystal Hollows only, draws each zone's border as a translucent colored wall: Jungle, Mithril Deposits, Goblin Holdout and Precursor Remnants (default colors are the Crystal Hollows Map's zone colors, Jungle purple, Mithril green, Goblin gold, Precursor blue, and the map fills its four quadrants with the colors set here; the four surface quadrants, split at X and Z 513), Magma Fields (the layer at or below Y 64 under the whole Hollows, so its outer walls and its top) and the Crystal Nucleus box (white by default; it sits on the edge of the protected middle, X 462 to 564 and Z 461 to 565, where Hypixel answers a dig with "A magical force surrounding this area prevents you from breaking blocks!"; the quadrants' inner walls stop at its edge and nothing is drawn inside it except its own walls). The bounds are the Hollows' own, X and Z 202 to 823 and Y 31 to 188 (not SkyHanni's looser warning box); the real biome edge is cave terrain, so a wall marks the nominal line. Each zone draws only its own side of a shared line, so two colors never fight over one plane. Per zone: Color, Opacity (5 to 100 percent, 25 default) and Near Only (the wall is clipped to a cube of 32 to 256 blocks around you, or Always for the whole wall; 96 default). Hide Walls In Structures (off by default) draws no walls while the sidebar names a structure (Mines of Divan, Lost Precursor City, Jungle Temple, Goblin Queen's Den, Khazad-dum, Fairy Grotto, Dragon's Lair). Only the nearest wall layer along a view ray is drawn, so a zone's color looks the same in front of another zone's wall as in front of open cave. Depth-tested; nothing draws outside the Crystal Hollows or outside Skyblock.
- Lockpick Helper — **off by default, both builds**. Hypixel shows the chest side to click with crit particles; while one of your treasure chests is boxed, this averages those particles, fills the chest face they are on and draws a small dot (Color) at the exact spot. It only draws; you aim and click yourself.
- Powder Tracker — **off by default**. A movable HUD on the mining islands: this session's chests, Gemstone, Mithril (Show Mithril Powder, on) and Glacite powder, each with a per-hour rate, the uptime and a 2x tag while a 2x Powder event runs. Show Top Drops (on) lists the five most common other rewards. Chests and drops are counted from Hypixel's own loot block ("CHEST LOCKPICKED" / "LOOT CHEST COLLECTED", then one line per reward), so each chest counts once. Powder is counted from the tab list's Powders widget: every rise of a total is a gain, so 2x Powder, Sky Mall, Heart of the Mountain perks and every source (chests, ores, Golden Goblins, the first ore of the day) count exactly as the profile got them; spending is not a loss, and a jump of over 100,000 in one read (a Heart of the Mountain reset) is not a gain. A powder type the widget does not show (the widget turned off in /tab) is counted from the loot lines instead, never both. Uptime counts the time between chests and powder gains, with any gap over three minutes taken as a break, so the rates hold while you are away. Reset Session starts over (so does a restart).
- 2x Powder Alert — **off by default**. A 2X POWDER title and a sound (Sound, on) when Hypixel says "2X POWDER STARTED!" on a mining island; a player's message saying it does nothing. The Powder Tracker shows the 2x tag until "2X POWDER ENDED!" or a world change.
- Chest Loot Compact — **off by default**. In the Crystal Hollows, Hypixel's multi-line loot block for each treasure chest is hidden and replaced by one line ("+537 Gemstone, +153 Mithril, +2 items"); hover it for every reward. A separator line that does not open a loot block is shown as normal. Show (left click forward, right click back) picks what the line lists: All (default), Powder Only, Powder + Rare (powder plus the rare drops: Fine and Flawless gemstones, Prehistoric Egg, Pickonimbus 2000, the under-1% entries of the wiki's Treasure Chest/Crystal Hollows Loot page) or Hide All (the block is hidden and nothing is printed). When a chest has nothing to show in Powder Only or Powder + Rare, nothing is printed. The Powder Tracker counts every chest in every mode, and whether this is on or off. **Hide Treasure Chest Uncovered** (on by default, chattidy-1104) hides the "You uncovered a treasure chest!" line from the chat window after the Treasure Chests tracker and the Treasure Chest Alert have read it. **Keep Rare Rewards** (on by default, from staging-mining-3's Powder Chest Chat Filter) leaves each rare reward's own line (the Powder + Rare list) in chat while Compact hides the rest of the block, in every Show mode.
- Profit Per Hour — watches what your inventory actually gains while mining (main inventory, armor and off-hand) and prices each gain from the mod's existing Bazaar and Auction House data, against a clock that pauses rather than resets. Mining Islands Only (on by default) runs the clock only on the Dwarven Mines, Glacite Tunnels, Crystal Hollows, Gold Mine and Deep Caverns. Items it cannot price are counted as unpriced, never as zero. Totals survive a restart; `/profit mining` opens them. Off by default, like every profit tracker.
- Nucleus Run Profit — counts your Crystal Nucleus runs from Hypixel's own loot bundle chat message and prices each reward line the same way Profit Per Hour does. The run count is exact; item values are best effort, a line it cannot price is skipped rather than guessed, and the key, Precursor Apparatus and robot part costs are not subtracted. Shows runs, coins per run, coins per hour and total value; `/profit nucleus` opens them. Off by default.
- Crystal Hollows Map — a top-down map of the Crystal Hollows with your live position, your own waypoints and the structures you have found. The Nucleus is the map's center and is never drawn as a waypoint or label. A structure appears only once you have stood inside it (the sidebar names the area): Show Borders draws a box around the part of it you have walked, Show Waypoints a named marker at its middle, both in the color of the zone it lies in (the Crystal Hollows Walls zone colors). Share My Finds sends your finds to other mod users in the same lobby over the mod's relay, and Receive Others' shows theirs with a dashed border; both on by default. The map is off by default; `/chmap` opens it full screen (left click sets a travel target, right click clears it; it never moves you). While it is on, the whole cave shows as a small movable, scalable HUD square in the Crystal Hollows (size it in Edit HUD): the zones, finds, waypoints, the travel target and you as an arrow. Show Zones tints the four zones on both maps. Names On HUD writes each structure's name beside its marker on the HUD; HUD Name Size (0.5x to 2x) scales those names, which sit on a dark backing and stack instead of overlapping. Under Waypoints, Add Waypoint Here saves where you stand; each saved waypoint has a name box, a Color button (the menu theme's accent until picked), Remove, and X / Y / Z boxes with a Set button (Enter saves too). Your waypoints also show in the world as labels that keep one readable size at any distance. Cheat build only, at the bottom under a red Cheat header: the Structure Scanner (its own switch, Structure Waypoints, Chat On Find, Show On Map, Fairy Grottos and a Scanner Toggle Keybind).
- Metal Detector — in the Mines of Divan, works out where the Metal Detector's treasure is from its action bar readout and marks it. It finds the mines' center from any Keeper armor stand, takes a reading only once the same distance shows twice from the same spot (the readout lags while you move), and narrows the 42 known chest spots down until one is left; then it draws a box on it and a line to it (Line To Treasure), through walls. Show Possible Spots outlines what is still possible, Chat Updates says how many are left, and both colors can be changed. A "You found" message starts the next search. It never moves you or clicks. Off by default.
- Mining Events — a title and a sound (Event Title, Event Sound, Event Sound Choice) when a Dwarven Mines or Crystal Hollows event is 20 seconds from starting, from Hypixel's "⚑ The ... event starts in 20 seconds!" line, and the Event Timer HUD: each running event with the time it has left, started by its STARTED line and stopped by its ENDED line or its known length (2x Powder 15 min, Goblin Raid 5 min, Raffle 160 s, Mithril Gourmand 10 min, Better Together and Gone With The Wind 18 min). Ending Soon alerts again at 20 seconds left. Each event has its own switch. Chat only; off by default.
- Fossil Solver — in the Glacite Fossil Excavator, counts every placement of the eight fossils (Claw, Tusk, Ugly, Helix, Webbed, Footprint, Clubbed, Spine, in the orientations Hypixel uses; shapes from Skyblocker) that agrees with the tiles already dug (white panes fossil, brown dirt unknown, anything else empty) and with the "Fossil Excavation Progress" percentage once one shows. The unknown tiles covered by the most placements get a green outline; with Show Chances the others get a blue fill as strong as their chance. Solver Info Line puts a line above the menu: placements left, the fossil once only one kind fits, and the fewest tiles left to dig, in red when that is more than the chisel charges shown. Draws only; you dig. Off by default.
- Lobby Tracker — remembers the mining lobbies you have been in, by the server id in the tab list, and when you join one again says how long ago and how many times (Seen Before Message). The Lobby HUD shows this lobby's id, your visits and, with Show World Day, the world's day counter (its tick count divided by 24000; the world's own clock, not a number Hypixel publishes). Up to 500 lobbies are kept, Forget Lobbies clears them, and the history stays out of profiles. Off by default.
- Crystal Tracker — in the Crystal Hollows, the tab list's Crystals widget on a HUD, each crystal colored by what the widget says (placed green, found yellow, still needed red), with a title once every crystal is found or placed (All Found Title). The widget's own words are read as they stand. Off by default.
- Cold Overlay — while the sidebar shows Hypixel's "Cold: -N❄" line (the Glacite Tunnels and Mineshafts), vanilla's powder-snow frost is drawn over the screen, more opaque the colder you are (N out of 100), scaled by Strength (10 to 100%, 100 by default). A campfire's "reduced your ❄ Cold to 0!" clears it at once. Draws only. Off by default.
- Fetchur Solver — in the Dwarven Mines, when Fetchur gives his daily riddle ("[NPC] Fetchur: its yellow and see through"), a chat line under it names the item he wants (Answer In Chat), and optionally a title (Answer Title). Twelve riddles are known (Skyblocker's list); an unknown one is said as unknown. Reads chat only. Off by default.
- Puzzler Solver — in the Dwarven Mines, when the Puzzler gives his ten arrows ("[NPC] Puzzler: ▲▶◀▼..."), walks them from his start block (181, 195, 135; up +z, down -z, left +x, right -x) and puts a green box on the block they end on (Show Box), with its coordinates in chat (Chat Coordinates). It only draws: the block in your world is never changed. The box moves with new arrows and goes after five minutes or a world change. Off by default.
- Sky Mall Display — each SkyBlock day Hypixel announces your Heart of the Mountain Sky Mall buff ("New buff: Gain +100⸕ Mining Speed."); a movable HUD keeps it, with the time until it changes (a SkyBlock day, 20 minutes, from the line). Mining Islands Only (on) shows it only where the buff works, and Alert On flashes a title when the new buff is the one you pick (Mining Speed, Mining Fortune, more Powder, Pickaxe Ability cooldowns, Goblin chance, Titanium). The Heart of the Forest's perks use the same "New buff:" words and are ignored. It waits for the next announcement after a restart. Reads chat only. Off by default.
- Deployables — off by default, legit (draws only). Finds placed lanterns by Hypixel's label armor stand over them, named "<Name> <seconds>s" (the counting name is the timer, its position the center every range check measures from), on the mining islands. Show Radius Circle (on) draws the area each one buffs, in Circle Color, and in In Radius Color while you stand inside it and get its effect; Range Style is Circle (a ring of the radius on the ground under it, with Circle Thickness and Circle Height, like Door Keys' circle) or Sphere (a see-through ball of the radius: inside it you get the effect). Timer HUD (on) lists each one with its time left and "(in range)" while you are inside; Timer Shows is Always or Only In Radius. Move the HUD in the HUD editor ("Deployable Timers"). A Glacite Lantern or Will-o'-wisp buffs the whole Glacite Mineshaft, so there it counts as in range everywhere. Other Deployables (off) also tracks power orbs, the Umberella and Black Holes, anywhere on Skyblock. Only one deployable buff applies at a time.

  | Deployable | Radius | Lasts | Buff (Hypixel SkyBlock wiki) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Dwarven Lantern | 30 | 5 min | +20 Mining Speed |
  | Mithril Lantern | 30 | 5 min | +40 Mining Speed, +10 Mining Fortune |
  | Titanium Lantern | 30 | 5 min | +60 Mining Speed, +15 Mining Fortune, +5 Heat Resistance |
  | Glacite Lantern | 30 (whole Mineshaft) | 5 min | +80 Mining Speed, +20 Mining Fortune, +10 Heat Resistance, +5 Cold Resistance |
  | Will-o'-wisp | 30 (whole Mineshaft) | 5 min | +100 Mining Speed, +25 Mining Fortune, +20 Heat Resistance, +10 Cold Resistance, +2.5 Gemstone Spread |
  | Radiant / Mana Flux / Overflux power orb | 18 | 30 s / 30 s / 60 s | healing, Health Regen, mana regen, Strength |
  | Plasmaflux power orb | 20 | 60 s | the most of each |
  | Umberella | 30 | 5 min | +5 Trophy Chance |
  | Black Hole (Small/Medium/Large Pocket) | 20 | 3 min | pulls low-health mobs, catches shards |

  Flares (Warning, Alert, SOS: 40 blocks, 3 min) carry no timer label, only a skull, and are not tracked; nor is the Totem of Corruption.
- Wishing Compass Solver — in the Crystal Hollows, a Wishing Compass shoots a trail of particles toward what it points at. The first 25 trail particles of a use give a direction from where you stood; a second use 8 or more blocks away gives another, and where the two lines pass within 5 blocks of each other is the target: its coordinates in chat, named by the zone you used it in (Jungle Temple or Odawa, Mines of Divan, Goblin King or Queen, Lost Precursor City, Khazad-dûm), and a box with a label there (Show Waypoint) until you change worlds. "The Wishing Compass can't seem to locate anything!" starts it over. Unlike Skyblocker's, it never cancels a use; it reads the particles and your right-click only. Off by default.
- Worm Alert — in the Crystal Hollows, Hypixel's "You hear the sound of something approaching..." (a Stoneworm or Scatha spawned by your mining) gets a "Worm!" title (Spawn Title) and a pling (Spawn Sound); with Scatha Alert, a "[Lv..] Scatha" nametag within 32 blocks in the next 10 seconds shows "SCATHA!". The Cooldown HUD counts the 30-second spawn cooldown down to "Worm ready" and keeps the session's worm and Scatha count. Reads chat and nametags only. Off by default.
- Structure Scanner — **cheat build only**, at the bottom of the Crystal Hollows Map tab under a red Cheat header (its own Structure Scanner switch, Structure Waypoints, Chat On Find, Show On Map, Fairy Grottos and a Scanner Toggle Keybind; its old card is gone, a saved toggle key carries over). The "illegal map" of the Crystal Hollows spec: reads every Crystal Hollows chunk you load, once per lobby (one per tick as they arrive, only the chunk sections whose palette holds a structure's first block), for each structure's unique column of blocks and marks what it finds: Goblin Queen's Den, Mines of Divan, Lost Precursor City, Jungle Temple, Goblin King, Bal (Khazad-dûm), Corleone Dock, Key Guardian Spiral, Odawa, Golden Dragon and Fairy Grottos (Fairy Grottos switch). Structure Waypoints outlines each spot through walls with the structure's name floating over it, Chat On Find names it, and Show On Map (off) adds every one, by name, to the Crystal Hollows Map as scanned; a scanned find is never shared with other players. Each find is placed at the center of the box its marker blocks cover within 32 blocks of the matched column (not at the column, which can sit at a stair entrance), and the waypoint, box and label take the color of the zone it lies in (the Crystal Hollows Walls zone colors). Crystal Nucleus is never marked. A new lobby, or switching it off and on, scans from scratch. It reads blocks only, never clicks or moves. Off by default.
- Ghost ESP — **cheat build only.** Outlines the Dwarven Mines' Ghosts, the invisible creepers of The Mist, through walls by default (Ghosts Through Walls), in a color you choose. It only draws. Off by default.

**Carnival** (its own menu category, carnival-1110, ships before 2.0; every switch starts OFF)

- Carnival Tracker — in the Hub's Carnival (Foxy's Chivalrous Carnival), a HUD with the game you are playing and its sidebar stats (score, fruits, catch streak, kills, accuracy, time left; Current Game), your Carnival Tokens and what this session added, the games you finished this session and the tokens the "You earned N Carnival Tokens!" lines gave (Session), your best score in each game kept between sessions (Best Scores), the Carnival Goals not yet reached, read when you open Chantelle's Carnival Goals menu or from the goal-complete chat lines (Goals Left), and a line while today's 25 Carnival Tickets from the Carnival Leader are unclaimed, with a chat reminder as you walk in (Ticket Reminder). Its history is kept out of profiles. Display only.

- Fruit Digging Solver — while you play Fruit Digging in the Carnival, keeps the 7x7 sand grid's state (which tiles are dug, what each held from the tag over it, the Mines, Treasure and Anchor clues each dig gave, the fruits a bomb destroyed, the tiles left standing beside a bomb that went off) and boxes the best tile to dig next in the color of the shovel mode to dig it with (Mines, Anchor or Treasure Color), labeled with the mode and its bomb odds (Best Dig). The odds come from sampling every board that fits the clues seen so far; the pick weighs expected points (Apple and Cherry growing, Pomegranate's x1.5 and Durian's x0.5 on the next dig, Watermelon's bonus reveal) against the chance of wasting the dig on a bomb or rum and of a bomb destroying fruit around it. Clue Labels puts each clue over its dug tile, Fruit Labels each known fruit, Tile Odds the bomb-or-rum odds on every unknown tile. The Solver HUD shows digs used of 15, the next dig's multiplier, the recommended tile (A1 is the corner at the lowest x and z) and mode, its bomb and rum odds and expected points, and the fruit still in the ground (Fruit Left). The grid is found as the 7x7 sand patch nearest you when the game starts. Click Guards (legit, they only cancel your own click, off by default): Prevent Misclicks cancels a dig on any grid tile but the best one, Prevent Wrong Shovel cancels a dig while the shovel is not in the best tile's mode, and Sneak To Dig Anyway (on by default) lets a sneaking dig through. Otherwise display only: it never digs for you.

- Zombie Shootout Helper — while you play Zombie Shootout in the Carnival, outlines each zombie in its armor's color (leather brown, iron gray, gold, diamond aqua; Zombie Outlines, or only the ones worth the most with Highest Only), puts a timer over each counting down its lifetime with its points (Zombie Timers; the wiki's 7, 6, 5 and 4 seconds, a baby Leather zombie worth double), boxes the best target - a lit Redstone Lamp first (100 points and 1.2x for 6 seconds), then the zombie worth the most and soonest gone (Best Target, Best Target Color), boxes each lit lamp (Lit Lamps) with a line to it (Lamp Line), and shows a HUD with the best target, the lamp's time left and the zombies up (Shootout HUD). Display only: it never shoots.

- Catch a Fish Helper — while you play Catch a Fish in the Carnival, outlines every fish in the pond, the 200-point Yellow Fish in their own color (Fish Outlines, Yellow Only, Yellow Fish Color, Other Fish Color), boxes the fish to go for - the nearest Yellow Fish, else the nearest fish (Best Fish) - with a line to it (Line to Fish), and shows a HUD with the fish and yellow fish up, your catch streak from the sidebar, HOOKED while your hook has a fish on it (hold Use to reel) and how long your streak lasts without a hook (the wiki's 3 seconds; Fish HUD). A Yellow Fish is told by Skyblocker's head texture; red and blue fish share the other color. Display only: it never casts or reels.

- Zombie Shootout Triggerbot — **cheat build only.** while you play Zombie Shootout holding the Carnival Dart Tube (Dart Tube Only), the moment your crosshair is on a zombie or a lit Redstone Lamp (ray-traced out to 64 blocks, nothing solid in front), taps your Use key, or Attack (Shoot With), once: after the Aim Delay on a new target (0-500 ms, 50 by default), then at most once per Shot Delay (100-1000 ms, 250 by default, each gap rolled up to a quarter longer). It never turns you: you aim, it clicks. One tap a tick through ActionGate at the start of the tick. Automation: can get you banned; keep it off on the main server. Off by default.

- Auto Zombie Shootout — **cheat build only.** while you play Zombie Shootout holding the Carnival Dart Tube (Dart Tube Only), turns your view to the best target in sight - a lit Redstone Lamp first (Lamps First), then the zombie worth the most and soonest gone - through Auto Routes' eased rotation, following it as it walks, and taps Use, or Attack (Shoot With), once your crosshair is on a target: after the Aim Delay on a new target, at most once per Shot Delay. Aim Mode: Legit turns your view in an eased, visible turn; No Rotate turns only the look the server hears while your screen holds still (ViewFreeze). Moving the mouse yourself stops it for the rest of that game, and so does the Emergency Cancel Key. It never moves you, one tap a tick through ActionGate, and a server correction is reported while it carries on. Automation: can get you banned; keep it off on the main server. Off by default.

- Auto Shovel Swap — **cheat build only.** while you play Fruit Digging, puts your Carnival Shovel in the mode the Fruit Digging Solver wants for the next dig (Mines, Anchor or Treasure, the color its Best Dig box is drawn in): a hotbar slot already holding a shovel in that mode is selected as a number key would, else, holding the Carnival Shovel, it taps Use to step the shovel to its next mode (the wiki's Carnival Shovel page: right click cycles Mines, Anchor and Treasure) until it is right, at most once per Swap Delay (100-2000 ms, 300 by default), giving up on a pick after three taps that change nothing. After a Rum the next dig has no ability, so it leaves the shovel alone. The mode is read from the held item's name, else its look (iron Mines, diamond Anchor, golden Treasure). One action a tick through ActionGate. Automation: can get you banned; keep it off on the main server. Off by default.

- Auto Fruit Digging — **cheat build only.** while you play Fruit Digging, turns your view through Auto Routes' eased rotation to the tile the Fruit Digging Solver picks and, once your crosshair is on it, taps your Attack key once to dig it, then waits for the tile to stop being sand before the next, at least Dig Delay apart (100-2000 ms, 500 by default). With Auto Shovel Swap on it first waits up to two seconds for the shovel to be in the solver's mode. It digs only tiles within 4.5 blocks of your eyes and never walks: stand in the middle of the grid, or step closer when it says a tile is out of reach. Free Camera keeps your screen still while your body turns; moving the mouse yourself stops it for the rest of that game. One tap a tick through ActionGate; a server correction is reported while it carries on. Automation: can get you banned; keep it off on the main server. Off by default.

- Auto Catch a Fish — **cheat build only.** while you play Catch a Fish holding the Carnival Rod, turns your view through Auto Routes' eased rotation to the Catch a Fish Helper's pick (the nearest Yellow Fish, else the nearest fish), following it as it swims, and taps Use to cast once it is within a few degrees; when your hook has a fish on it, holds your Use key down to reel it (the wiki's hold right click) until the fish is off the hook, then aims at the next. A cast that hooks nothing within Hook Wait (1-10 s, 3 by default) is reeled back with one tap and cast again; casts are at least Cast Delay apart (100-2000 ms, 300 by default). Free Camera keeps your screen still; moving the mouse yourself stops it for the rest of that game. It never moves you, taps and the hold go through ActionGate, and a server correction is reported while it carries on. Automation: can get you banned; keep it off on the main server. Off by default.

- Fruit Digging Triggerbot — **cheat build only.** while you play Fruit Digging, the moment your crosshair is on the tile the Fruit Digging Solver picks, and the shovel is in that tile's mode, it taps your Attack key once after Reaction Delay (100-2000 ms, 150 by default). You aim, it digs: it never turns you and never digs another tile. A dig goes only while the look the server last heard strikes the tile (GrimAC RotationBreak). One tap a tick through ActionGate. Automation: can get you banned; keep it off on the main server. Off by default.

**Diagnostics**

**General**
- Auto Join Skyblock — automatically runs `/skyblock` the moment you connect to Hypixel
- Etherwarp Overlay — while holding a real Etherwarp item, highlights exactly where you'd land (green if safe, red if not by default - both colors and Outline / Filled / Filled+Outline style are configurable) using a real voxel raycast. Never moves you — the real server still handles the actual warp (in General since 2026-10-04; it used to sit in Helpers). On p3sim.net it casts from Minecraft 1.8.9's sneaking eye height (1.54 instead of 1.27), which is what that server uses, so the highlight matches where p3sim actually puts you; everywhere else nothing changes. Auto Routes' etherwarp aims follow the same rule on p3sim. Show Through Blocks (off by default) draws the box through blocks in front of the target so the whole block shows. Crosshair On Server Aim (on by default) moves your crosshair onto the spot the server's ray meets while the overlay shows: on p3sim that higher eye puts the box above where the plain crosshair points, and the crosshair now sits on it; on Hypixel the two eyes agree and it does not move. A carpet is solid to the ray, as on Hypixel: a look at a carpet lands on the carpet itself, and a block with a carpet on top is no landing (the carpet fills the space your feet need). Auto Routes, Zero Ping Etherwarp and the dungeon sim's own etherwarp use the same rule, so on the sim a warp onto a carpet lands exactly where the box shows. In Outline style only the edges of the faces turned toward you are drawn, so the box never shows through a stair's or slab's open part. The box is the target block's own shape (a chest's, a slab's, a carpet's) unless Full Block Box is on, and it shows the same on chests, ender chests and trapped chests as on any block: until 2026-10-15 a chest's model was drawn over it on 26.1.2 and only slivers at its corners were visible
- Auto Sprint — **both builds, off by default** (Misc; 2026-11-01). While you walk forward yourself (and are not sneaking) the sprint key reads as held, exactly as if you held it; vanilla still decides whether you can sprint (hunger, blindness, using an item). It never touches the mod's own movement: while AP3, Auto Routes, the pathfinder (Interactive Map walks, Auto Fairy Souls, Auto Clear's walks) or AP3's freeze own your keys, their own sprint choice stands.
